# The dashboard

## 1. Purpose

The dashboard is where all three audiences meet Sekhemet. For **developers** it is a board that reads like Jira, Linear or GitHub Projects and a review instrument that gets a decision in under a minute. For **beginners** it teaches the practice where it happens, through a Learn layer they can switch off. For **non-developers** it is a status page in plain words and a conversation with the project manager that can start a project — without a terminal. For a **team** sharing one install it adds sign-in, the Projects page, an inbox and the pages an Admin needs ([teams](teams.md), [DEC-35](../DECISIONS.md#dec-35--one-product-two-setups-solo-and-team)). It serves the spine by making **the human the rate limiter** visible (Review WIP, back-pressure, *Needs you*) and by never letting the interface claim more than the evidence says. Behaviour behind the views lives in [planner-pm](planner-pm.md), [review-git](review-git.md), [design-stage](design-stage.md) and [integrations](integrations.md); the HTTP API is [runtime](runtime.md); PM shapes are [PM_CONTRACT](../PM_CONTRACT.md).

## 2. Behaviour

### 2.1 Stance

1. Every screen answers, in order: **What needs me? Can I trust it? What do I do?** That order sets the navigation, the default route and the hierarchy inside each view.
2. **State is colour; everything else is neutral.** Colour is reserved for the five state roles, and each is paired with an icon and words. Colour is never the only signal.
3. **Density over whitespace, evidence first, keyboard-complete, both themes first-class** (Basalt dark is the default; Sand light is tuned, not inverted). The theme is a choice kept per browser, made in the account menu (§2.2.6): *Dark* (Basalt), *Light* (Sand) or *System*, which follows the operating system's `prefers-color-scheme`; otherwise the operating system does not pick it.
4. **Sentence case everywhere**; no all-caps letter-spaced labels. **Gold (`--accent`) appears in exactly three places**: the active-nav indicator, the focus ring and the brand mark's sun disc. **The primary action is dark ink, never gold** (Accept, and the one primary of every view, §2.13.7; DEC-51, which settles SPEC-07: this rule and §2.13.7 said gold while §2.13's standard drew the dark button, and the later ruling wins).
5. **Every number has a unit and a basis** ("8 of 40 steps", "waiting 12m", "16.8k tokens in"). **Nothing is generic**: empty states say what a view is for and what fills it; errors say what failed, why, and what to do.
6. **Nothing the interface says outruns the evidence**: an empty gate contract, an altered ledger, a gate that did not run, a requirement whose tests pass without meeting their strength rule are shown as such. No model's confidence, and no model's claim that work is complete, is ever displayed.

### 2.2 Shell and navigation

1. **Grouped, labelled navigation** (sidebar ≥ 768 px), the same in the Solo and Team setups ([teams](teams.md) item 4), top to bottom:

| Group | Items (route · chord) | Visible when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Top | The brand mark and wordmark (§2.13.6); **Search**, which opens the palette (`⌘K`) | Always |
| Workspace | **Projects** `#/projects` · `g w`; **Inbox** `#/inbox` · `g x`; **My issues** `#/my-issues` · `g y` | Projects and Inbox: always. My issues: Team only |
| Project | The **project switcher** (the project's initials badge and *Chronicle ▾*, as the mockups draw it; the name alone while the person can see one project; item 7), then **Status** `#/status` · `g s`; **Board** `#/board` · `g b`; **Review** `#/review` · `g r`; **Project manager** (*Seshat* as secondary text) `#/pm` · `g p`; **Insights** `#/insights` · `g i` | Always (Insights shows its "not enough data" state until 3 cards finish) |
| More (collapsible) | **Runs** `#/runs` · `g u`; **Dependencies** `#/graph` · `g d`; **Playbook** `#/playbook` · `g k`; **Integrations** `#/integrations` · `g n`; **Machine** `#/machine` · `g m`; **Activity log** `#/ledger` · `g l` (DEC-52; *Ledger* before it, and the route and chord stay) | Runs: after the first completed run. Dependencies: when a card has `dependsOn`. Playbook: when the first rule or suggestion exists. Integrations, Machine and Activity log: always |
| Bottom | The **Agent status line** (item 3); **Configuration** `#/configuration` · `g c`; the **account** (avatar and name), which opens the account menu (§2.2.6) | Always |

   A view with nothing in it is **hidden, not empty**; the sidebar never shows a dead link. Items that count something show a count badge (Inbox: unread; Review: cards waiting; Playbook: rules awaiting approval). **Inbox is a page again** (superseded by [DEC-35](../DECISIONS.md#dec-35--one-product-two-setups-solo-and-team), which reverses the earlier merge into Review): it lists what reached the person across projects, grouped by reason (§2.17.2); Review › *Needs you* stays the project's queue of decisions, and an Inbox *Needs you* item opens there. **Projects** replaces the Workspace rollup, and `#/workspace` opens it (§2.11). Machine and Ledger move from the old System footer menu into More; Theme and Keys move into the account menu. Pages reached only from the account menu (Profile, Notifications, Members, Audit) are in the palette and have no chord. **Registry** and **Settings** are merged into **Configuration** (§2.16): `#/registry` opens its Benchmark section and `#/settings` its This browser section.
2. **Widths.** ≥ 1280 px: sidebar 216 px. 1024–1279 px: a 176 px sidebar that **keeps its labels** (no icon-only rail; the `--rail-w` token is retired). < 768 px: no sidebar; a **bottom tab bar — Status · Review · Board · PM** — with 48 px targets. Topbar 44 px (16 px padding, 12 px gaps): view title, crumb, scope filters, ⌘K, live status.
3. **Status at the bottom of the sidebar**, text plus one dot: the **Agent status line** — the Agent's state in one line (*Agent working on CHR-12 · step 14 of 40*, *Agent idle*, *Worker paused after step 5 while Seshat replies*, lapis, while it is), with the queue in the Team setup (*2nd in queue, about 6 minutes*, [teams](teams.md) item 31); under it, in 11 px secondary, *Live* / *Reconnecting…* / *Offline since 14:42*, *Activity log intact · 236 entries* (DEC-52) and memory. Who you are is the account menu (§2.2.6), not a footer line (superseded by DEC-35). Memory turns amber only when it changes behaviour (a pause is imminent or active), never as a permanent state.
4. **Shell bars** (one at a time, priority: ledger altered > offline > no Worker model > memory pause > review full > Worker paused for Seshat), each 32 px under the topbar:

| State | Trigger | Treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loading | Before the first board response | Skeleton of the real geometry, no spinner; after 3 s *Connecting to Sekhemet…* |
| Reconnecting | Stream error | Amber dot; content stays; actions enabled for 10 s |
| Offline | No stream for > 10 s and `/api/meta` fails | A full-width 32 px bar under the topbar in `--bg-raised` with a 1 px `--state-parked` bottom border: **Offline since 14:42:30.** *Showing the last known state. Actions are disabled.* `Retry`. Every triage button is disabled with the reason *Offline* beside it; timestamps freeze (they don't count up) |
| Read-only | `meta.triage === false` | Triage replaced by one line: **Read-only.** *This server was started without triage. Restart with `sekhemet serve` to accept or request changes.* (DEC-52) `a/r/p` show the same line as a toast |
| Review full | `backpressureActive` | **Review is full (3 of 3).** *Finished cards wait until you clear one.* `Open review` |
| No Worker model | The Worker role has no assigned model that fits this machine | **No model is set up for the Worker yet.** *Sekhemet can plan and show the board, but nothing is built until the Worker has a model.* `Set up models` (opens Configuration › Models) |
| Memory pause | Level critical, or the last stop was `memory_pressure` | **Paused for memory: 94% used.** *Sekhemet stopped the Worker safely before the system would swap. Work resumes below 85%.* `Machine`; the running tile flips to *Paused for memory* |
| Ledger altered | Chain verification fails | Red, not dismissible: **Activity log altered at entry #7.** *An entry no longer matches its hash. Stop and inspect before accepting anything.* `Open activity log` (DEC-52). Accept is disabled everywhere with that reason |
| Worker paused for Seshat | `PmStatus.workerPaused` | **Worker paused after step 5 while Seshat replies.** `Open Seshat`. Lapis rule, not amber: nothing is wrong |

5. **First run and default route.** The first visit asks one question: *I write code · I manage the work · I'm learning*. It sets the default route and the Learn default — *I write code*: Review when its queue is non-empty, else Board, Learn off; *I manage the work*: Status, Learn off; *I'm learning*: Board, Learn on; the answer is kept per browser and changeable in Configuration › Preferences (DEC-31: *Preferences*, never "This browser"). A fourth answer, *I'll just talk to Seshat*, opens Seshat (FINDINGS SHL-03). The question is a bar above the view that blocks nothing and takes no focus, under a one-line welcome, with *Not now* beside the answers; answering on the page the dashboard opened on takes the person to that answer's page. With no answer, a person who has never accepted a card lands on Status. **In the Team setup a person's profile label sets the default route instead** — a Product owner or a Stakeholder lands on Status, a Developer on the Board, or on My issues when they have any ([teams](teams.md) item 8) — and the question sets only Learn. **When no model is set up — no role's weights are found in any model folder — `sekhemet` opens the dashboard on Configuration › Models instead of the default route, on the first run and on every later run until a model is set up** ([surface](surface.md) item 5b; §2.16), and the welcome names the model step while the role question is asked at once (*FINDINGS SHL-03, C2a: before it the question waited for a model, so a Stakeholder or Viewer, who never sets one up, was never asked*); only Solo's person or a Team Admin is sent to Configuration › Models, and anyone else lands on their own page under the *No Coding model* bar. With some roles set up but not the Worker, the default route stands and the *No Coding model* bar says what is missing.

6. **The account menu** (the avatar and name at the bottom of the sidebar; replaces "the footer shows who you are", §2.15.7). Its header shows the person's initials avatar, name and email — in Solo, the name and *This computer* ([teams](teams.md) item 1). Items: **Profile** (name, initials avatar, email, profile label, and in Team the personal access tokens, [teams](teams.md) item 15); **Notifications** (which reasons reach the person by email or chat, within the notification budget, [integrations](integrations.md) items 21–23a); **Keyboard shortcuts** (the cheat sheet, §2.3.3); **Theme** — *System*, *Light*, *Dark* (§2.1.3). In the Team setup also **Members** (and **Audit** for an Admin; §2.17), and **Sign out**. **Switch workspace** is in both setups (item 7; [DEC-57](../DECISIONS.md#dec-57--a-server-is-a-workspace-with-many-projects), the later ruling — before it, Team only, *another Sekhemet server the person has signed in to*). Solo shows no Members, Audit or Sign out.

7. **The project switcher and the workspace switcher** (NEW-dashboard-25; [DEC-57](../DECISIONS.md#dec-57--a-server-is-a-workspace-with-many-projects); FINDINGS SHL-01). A server is a workspace of many projects ([teams](teams.md) item 5).
   - **The project switcher** heads the Project group. It shows the current project's initials badge and name, never the repository's path: the path is in its tooltip and in Configuration › Project (SHL-01). Pressing it opens a list of the projects the person can see, from `GET /api/projects/overview`, each with its badge, name and state, filtered as the person types, with **New project** at its foot for a person who may create one ([teams](teams.md) item 6). Choosing a project makes it the current project of every project page and keeps the person on the same kind of page (Board stays Board). The choice is kept per browser, like the theme; a link to an issue makes that issue's project current. The palette has *Switch project…*.
   - **The workspace switcher** is the account menu's *Switch workspace* (item 6), in both setups. It lists the workspaces `GET /api/workspaces` returns — each with its name, its address and *Solo* or *Team*, the current one marked ([runtime](runtime.md) item 23c) — and choosing one opens that address in this tab, where that workspace's own sign-in applies ([teams](teams.md) item 5a). In Solo, *Add a workspace…* takes another workspace's address and *Remove* drops an entry from the list. A Team server lists only itself, and offers *Open another workspace…*, which takes an address and opens it. The palette has *Switch workspace…*.

### 2.3 Keyboard

1. Three tiers: **navigation and triage** (learned in a day), the **command palette** (`⌘K`/`Ctrl+K`; anything in it needs no shortcut, and a palette entry is the default for a new action), and **`g` chords**, one per *visible* view. A chord is the **first letter** of the view's name where that letter is free; where it is taken, the keymap names a fixed alternative: `g u` Runs, `g k` Playbook, `g n` Integrations, `g x` Inbox, `g y` My issues; Projects keeps Workspace's `g w`. Configuration is `g c`. The previous chords (`g a` PM, `g f` Insights, `g q` Runs, `g e`, and `g ,`, which opened Settings and now opens Configuration › This browser) keep working silently for one release; `g s`, `g p` and `g i`, which meant Integrations, Playbook and Inbox before, take their new meanings at once and the cheat sheet says so for one release.
2. **No destructive or surprising action has a bare single-key binding**: there is no bare `t` for theme; theme and Learn are toggled from the palette, the account menu and Configuration › This browser.
3. **The cheat sheet (`?`) is the specification**: it and the palette are generated from one keymap, and an action not in it has no shortcut. It is a 720 px-wide modal that fits a 900 px-high window (scrolling inside), in four columns: Global, Navigate, Cards, Review. Keys that do not apply to the current view are dimmed.
4. Keys are ignored while focus is in a text field, except `Esc` and `⌘↵`.

| Where | Keys |
| --- | --- |
| Global | `⌘K` palette (`>` commands, `#` cards; no match offers *Ask Seshat: <query>*) · `?` keys · `g` + letter · `⌘J` Seshat panel · `Esc` close/back |
| Board | `h/l` columns, keeping the row index, clamped to the target column's length; `j/k` cards (cross lane boundaries; `h/l` stay in the lane) · a click on a tile opens its peek, a Shift-, ⌘- or Ctrl-click selects it (as `x`; BRD-06); `Space` peeks · `Enter` open · `x` select · `c` new card · `v` board/list · `⇧V` pipeline stages · `/` filter · `⇧S` cycle grouping · `Home/End` column ends |
| Board and list, focused card or selection | `⇧P` priority · `⇧E` points · `⇧L` labels · `⇧C` cycle · `⇧A` assignee (DEC-52) · `.` any field · in menus `1–9`, `↑↓`, `↵` · `z` undo the change the newest toast offers to undo (§2.4.23) |
| Triage view (§2.4.19) | `1` accept into Backlog · `2` decline · `3` duplicate of · `H` snooze · `j/k` rows |
| List | `x` · `⇧J/⇧K` extend · `⌘A` all visible · `Esc` clear · shift-click range · `Space` on a group header collapses it |
| Review and card | `a` accept · `r` request changes · `p` put on hold (DEC-52; the keys keep their letters) · `z` undo accept (grace window), or the change the newest toast offers to undo · `x` acknowledge the focused finding · `j/k` next/previous · `o`/`Enter` open card · `[` `]` attempts · `f` facts rail · `u` unified/split · `n/N` next/previous annotation · `Space` expand file · `1–5` card tabs |
| Composer | `↵` send · `⇧↵` newline · `@` mention an issue — in an issue's comment box also a person or an AI teammate ([teams](teams.md) item 23) · `Esc` returns focus to the page without closing the panel |
| Proposals | `y` apply · `n` discard · `⇧Y` apply all in the group · `j/k` move |
| Decision request | `1–9` pick · `↵` answer |

### 2.4 Board

1. **Five professional columns over the nine stored states** (the same mapping the Jira export uses, so the board and the export agree; the names are [NAMING](../NAMING.md)'s):

| Column | Stored states | Status badge on the tile |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Backlog** | backlog | — |
| **To do** | ready, planning | *Being planned* (pencil) · *Blocked · waits on X* |
| **In progress** | in_progress, verify | *Editing src/hasher.ts* (running; the step count is the issue's, never the tile's: DB-N7-3) · *Running Tests…* (naming the check that is running) · *Waiting for AI review* · *Types failed · retrying (rung 1 of 4)* (fail) · *Paused for Seshat* |
| **In review** | review | *4 of 4 checks passed · waiting 2h* (amber after 2 h) · *Accepted · PR #n open* (held `awaitingMerge`, not counted against the limit, [review-git §2.5.7](review-git.md)). The Review WIP limit sits here |
| **Done** | done | *Merged ba1338e · 2h ago* |
| *On hold* (only when non-empty) | parked | *Paused for memory*, *Looping · parked*, *Decision needed*, or the person's note |
| *Won't do* (a filter, not a column) | rejected | — (split parents read *Split into 3 cards*) |

   Verify still gates entry to In review (a transition, not a column). **Pipeline stages** (`⇧V`) splits To do and In progress back into the nine machine columns for operators, named as stored, in sentence case (Backlog, Ready, Planning, In progress, Verify, Review, Done, Parked, Rejected; [NAMING](../NAMING.md)); the choice is kept per browser. Moving a card past a failing check is never a board gesture: a check override (a gate override, internally) is a recorded human decision with a reason, made from the CLI or the API ([kernel](kernel.md)); the card view shows that it happened.
2. **Columns.** Fluid (`min 200px`, `max 300px`, 8 px between columns and 8 px between tiles; 8 px body padding, 16 px board padding). **Empty columns collapse into chips above the board** (*Done 4 ›*), never rotated rails; Done is a full column whenever it has cards. **Every column stays in view where the columns fit, and none is ever drawn over another** (FINDINGS BRD-01): at 1280 px and wider, columns that do not fit at 200 px relax to 184 px (the dock's own minimum) rather than scroll; where they still do not fit, In review and On hold sit in **a pane of their own at the right**, behind a hairline, and the other columns scroll beside it — the person's queue is never the part that gets scrolled away, and it covers no column. Until a person scrolls, the board opens on the first working column (In progress where it shows, else the first after Backlog) one column gap from the left edge, or on the column before it where the scroll ends first, so no column opens cut. Below 768 px it is one column with a column switcher (§2.14.3). The layout rule is `boardFit` and `openColumn` (`reach.ts`), the same the reach check (DB-P3-17) lays out. Once more than six columns hold cards (as in Pipeline stages), the scrolling columns beyond the viewport are windowed horizontally. Columns have no box of their own: a column is its header over its tiles, one level of container (§2.13, FINDINGS BRD-10, the approved board mockup). Cards in a column keep their recorded order (a fractional `order_key`, [kernel](kernel.md)), except In review and On hold, which sort by wait time, longest first.
3. **Column header** (36 px, 0 12 px padding): name · `count / limit` for WIP-limited columns · the column's points sum (*13 pts*), only when Preferences → Estimation is *Story points* (DB-N7-2) · a 2 px capacity bar (`--text-secondary`; amber at capacity; red over) · a Tips *?* when Tips are on · a `⋯` menu (sort by priority, wait time or recently changed; collapse). The In review limit is always shown with its derivation (*limit 3, from 60 review minutes a day at ~20 min per card*); no limit is hidden for being large. At capacity the header says, as visible text in its popover, *Full. The agent holds finished cards until you clear one.* On hold, when it holds cards, sits far right and shows its count in the parked tone (`--state-parked`).
4. **Tile anatomy** (88 px compact; 112 px comfortable adds the first line of the issue's description, and only that — no budget is on a tile, DB-N7-3, and every place that describes the density says so, FINDINGS BRD-13; the heights are fixed because the board's windowing depends on them; width follows the column; 8/12 px padding, 4 px row gap, 6 px radius):

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ◇ CHR-12              3 pts  JD  Agent   │ row 1: issue type icon · issue key · points (only with estimation on) · assignee (a person's avatar) · delegate chip
│ Implement canonical JSON and SHA-256     │ row 2: title 13/500, 2-line clamp
│ ▮▮▯  Ledger  api  security  3 subtasks   │ row 3: priority glyph (hidden when none) · epic chip · ≤2 labels, then +n · subtasks
│ ✕ Types failed · 3 errors · 22h          │ row 4: status badge (icon + colour + words) · check pips · work item age
└──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

   - **Issue type** (DEC-31): the icon and word of the standard issue type — *Story* (new behaviour), *Bug* (a fix), *Task* (a refactor, upgrade or characterization, a review card, or a card of the `task` tier), *Spike* (a spike or research card), *Epic* (an epic; an initiative keeps *Initiative*) — derived from the stored `kind`, `change` and tier by `issueTypeOf` (`vocabulary.ts`). The internal kind labels (Contract, Storage, Flow, Rules) are retired from every screen.
   - **Points** show on the tile, the column header, swimlane and group headers, the bulk bar, the list's Points column and the reports only when the project's **Preferences → Estimation** is *Story points* (DB-N7-2); with it *Off* (the default) no points show, the sprint header and burn-up count issues, and the *Unestimated* view and filter are not offered.
   - **Assignee and delegate** (DEC-52: *Assignee* on screen, as Jira, Linear and GitHub name the field; the stored field stays `owner`). A person stays the card's **assignee** and may have an avatar (a 20 px monogram of their initials); the agent, or another person, is the **delegate** that builds it, shown as a **text chip** (*Agent*, or the person's name) — the convention Linear, Cursor, Codex and Copilot use. **A role never has an avatar** ([NAMING](../NAMING.md)). The fields are [kernel](kernel.md)'s (`owner`, `delegate`); the export mapping is [integrations](integrations.md)'s.
   - **The status never truncates its cause**; it may wrap to two lines inside the 88 px by dropping row 3's labels.
   - **Blocked.** A card with a `blockedReason` or an unfinished dependency carries a **blocker flag in the fail tone, with the link icon and the word *Blocked*** and its cause (*Blocked · waits on @hasher*); colour is never the only signal.
   - One badge is icon + colour + words, and **never combines a stage with a failure mark** (no "✕ Planning"): it says what is true now (*Needs a new plan*).
   - **Priority glyph**: a fixed 12 px slot; three rising bars (1–3 lit, unlit bars in `--border-strong`, stroke 3 so the icon test holds) for Low/Medium/High in `--text-secondary`, a boxed exclamation for Urgent in `--text-primary`; the shape, not colour, carries priority. No glyph for *No priority*; the list's Priority column is where priorities scan as a column.
   - **Epic chip** uses one of eight muted epic hues (chrome stays neutral). **Labels**: 11 px secondary text in a 1 px `--border-subtle` outline. Row 3 wraps rather than cut a chip until it says nothing (FINDINGS BRD-11).
   - **Check pips**: one 12×12 box per check the card's evidence ran, in `gates.toml` order — up to six; with more, one pip in the worst state and the count passed of ran (*11/13*), whose words say each state (*11 of 13 checks passed, 2 skipped*), so the status beside it is never squeezed (FINDINGS BRD-02); the status takes its own line before it would break a word — — ✓ passed, ✕ failed, – skipped, ring running, empty not run — with no letters; each glyph is a 1.5 px line in `--on-state` on the state fill for passed and failed, and *not run* is a 1 px `--border-strong` outline; and **never a check the evidence did not run** (no synthesised *Parse: pass*); the popover (focus or hover) lists each check's name, state, duration and first error line.
   - **No step counter and no model name on the tile** (DEC-31, DB-N7-3): the step budget (*8 of 40 steps*) and its bar are the issue's (its Steps tab and Facts), and model names are Configuration's and Machine's. Difficulty moves to the peek drawer and Facts.
   - **Work item age** shows on every In progress and In review tile (Kanban Guide), turning amber past the 85th-percentile cycle time.
   - **Tile states**: hover `--bg-overlay`; focus the global 2 px accent outline, offset 1 px (§2.14.5; the column body's 8 px padding keeps it unclipped); selected (`x`) a `--border-strong` border and a checkbox; focus and selection are visibly distinct and can co-exist. Done tiles are secondary text with no bars.
5. **Moves between columns happen only through recorded, gated actions** (triage, decisions, proposals); there is no drag between columns. **Reordering within a column** (drag or keys) is allowed and is recorded as one event.
6. **Peek drawer** (a click on the tile or `Space`, 480 px; BRD-06, as Linear, Jira and GitHub Projects open an issue on click): gates, failures, outcome, *Acceptance criteria* (DEC-52), kind and difficulty, assignee, delegate and suggested accepters ([review-git §2.4](review-git.md)), a compact file list, and triage keys that work inside it. `Enter` opens the card view; `Esc` closes the drawer and returns focus to the tile.
7. **Quick create** (`c`, the `+` on a column, or **New issue** in the board's top bar, beside **Ask Seshat** — the board's one primary action, §2.13, FINDINGS BRD-09; not on a phone, §2.14.3): a one-line title and optional description that goes through the planner pipeline as a create proposal ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md)) and is shown for Apply. No path ends in "use the CLI". The form also offers the issue's properties and, for a Bug, its reproduction (item 22); a Stakeholder files through the same form (item 19).
8. **Live updates** patch only changed tiles, keyed by id; they never re-render the board or reset scroll, focus or an open drawer; a focused card that changes column keeps focus and is scrolled into view, and no other card scrolls; a tile that changed shows *just now* for 10 s; no motion. A change made through the API (a park by `curl`) appears within 1 s. **A reload rebuilds the same state** from `/api/board` and the stream's replay (`?since=`, `Last-Event-ID`; `server.ts:574-580`), so what a person sees never depends on having watched live.
9. **Scale.** Windowed columns and cards (an overscan of 3 cards) keep 500 synthetic cards scrolling with no main-thread task over 50 ms and under 50 MB of DOM memory. Swimlanes are not windowed (they are for filtered views).
10. **Board states:** *No cards yet* with a **Start a project** button (opens the start page, [design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.11; DEC-51 — Seshat's panel before it) and beside it **Take over a project** (opens Seshat with the take-over conversation, for a repository someone started and did not finish: [design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.10, DS-TO-16; [DEC-43](../DECISIONS.md#dec-43--take-over-a-project)), and a secondary *or plan a feature* line; empty-column copy at the top of each column (Backlog *Ideas and split-off work.* · To do *Cards whose dependencies are done, and cards being planned.* · In progress *No agent running.* · In review *Nothing waiting for you.* · Done *Accepted cards appear here.* · On hold *Nothing parked.*); *No cards match "Type: Bug"* with `Clear filter`; review full and memory pause as shell bars, with *Holding for review* on held tiles. In Pipeline stages the machine columns keep their own empty copy: Ready *Cards whose dependencies are done.* · Planning *The planning model is writing plans and tests.* · Verify *Nothing being checked.*
11. **View bar** (40 px under the topbar, on `--bg-base` with a hairline; 16 px padding, 8 px gaps): `[Board | List]`, **View** menu (*All cards*, the default, *Current sprint*, *Needs you*, *Urgent and high*, *Unestimated* — only with estimation on —, *Triage* in the Team setup (item 19), and saved views, each with its query in mono), filter chips (click one to edit it, `✕` to remove it), `+ Filter` (Priority, Label, Epic, Sprint, Assignee, Delegate, Type, State; *Assignee* by DEC-52), the query box, **Group** (None, Epic, Assignee, Priority, Sprint), a density toggle, and **Save view** (shown only when the filter differs; saves `{ name, query, group, layout }` per browser until a views endpoint exists, and the menu says *Saved in this browser*).
12. **Query language** (GitHub Projects syntax): `priority:urgent,high`, `label:api`, `epic:ledger`, `sprint:current|none` (`cycle:` and `iteration:` are aliases), `assignee:@me|<name>` (`owner:`, the stored field's name, is its alias; DEC-52 makes *assignee* the word, where the alias ran the other way before), `delegate:agent|<name>` (`worker`, the older word, still matches), `type:story|task|bug|spike|epic` (`kind:` is the older spelling), `is:blocked|needs-you|running|unestimated|untriaged`, free words, which match title, key, description, acceptance criteria and comment text (item 21; title and key only before DEC-51), `-` to negate; terms AND, values OR; chips and text are two views of one filter object (`parseQuery` / `formatQuery`).
13. **Sprint header** (56 px, two lines, a 6 px progress bar; a sprint is the internal *cycle*, DEC-31) when a sprint is **in force** — the active sprint, started with *Start sprint* (item 20, DEC-51), or, while no sprint is active, a planned sprint whose dates contain today, whose header then offers **Start sprint** — unless the filter points at another sprint or `sprint:none`; the active sprint's header also offers **Complete sprint**: name, goal, dates, days left; one progress bar split — by issues, or by points with estimation on — into done (`--state-pass`), in progress (`--state-running`) and not started (`--bg-overlay`); a tick at the linear pace (elapsed days ÷ total days); *4 days left* turns amber with fewer than 2 days left and under 70% done (*Behind the linear pace by 2 issues*, or *by 5 pts* with estimation on, in visible text on focus); with estimation on, unestimated cards count as 1 point and the header says how many (*2 unestimated, counted as 1 pt*); **Plan next sprint with Seshat** opens the panel with that prompt filled in, not sent.
14. **Swimlanes** by Epic (with its progress bar, `done / total` cards), Assignee (DEC-52), Priority or Sprint; each lane header (32 px) has count, and points with estimation on; the *No epic* (etc.) lane is last; lanes collapse.
15. **List view** (`#/board/list`, `v`): it uses the same filter, grouping and selection as the board. A real table with columns: a leading selection checkbox · Priority · Key · Title (with issue type) · State · Epic · Sprint · Points (only with estimation on) · Labels · Assignee (DEC-52) · Delegate · Due · Updated; 36 px rows; the title fluid, other columns fixed, numbers right-aligned; group headers (32 px) with count and points; headers are buttons (Tab-reachable) that sort, and a second click reverses (stable, priority as tiebreak); inline edits — click a Priority, Epic, Sprint, Points, Labels, Assignee, Delegate or Due cell, or press its key on the focused row — with a menu anchored to the cell; the **Labels** menu is a checkable list with a *Create label "…"* row; **Due** offers *Today*, *End of this week*, *End of the current sprint*, *No due date* and a date; edits are optimistic, revert on failure and say why, and an applied edit offers *Undo* (item 23); `externalRef` links to the issue. A selected row has a 2 px `--text-primary` left bar.
16. **Bulk bar** (48 px, bottom centre, 16 px from the bottom, radius 6, `--bg-overlay` with a 1 px `--border-strong` edge) with the selection's count, and points with estimation on (*3 selected · 8 pts*), the field actions with their keys (*Priority ⇧P · Points ⇧E* (estimation on only) *· Sprint ⇧C · Labels ⇧L · Assignee ⇧A*) — the same menus as inline editing, applied to every selected card —, **Put on hold** (one reason, one hold per issue; DEC-52), **Ask Seshat** (the selection as mentions, not sent) and `✕ Esc`; one toast reports the result (*Set on 2 of 3. hasher: the server returned 409.*) and offers *Undo* for what was applied (item 23).
17. **Story map** (`#/board/map`): the backbone of user activities across in user order, the releases as horizontal bands beneath (*Release 1 · title*, *Release 2*; the first marked *First release* — *walking skeleton* is said only in Tips, DEC-31), and each requirement in its release with its state: **done** (proven on main), **tests too weak** (passing, strength unmet), **planned**, **unplanned**, **needs re-checking** (suspect: revised since its cards or tests were linked) or **cut**, each an icon plus words (the on-screen words are DEC-52's; the stored states keep their names) ([planner-pm §2.15](planner-pm.md); the requirement graph, walking skeleton and release slices are [PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH](../../research/PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH.md)'s). Cards keep their tiles and states under their requirement. The story map is a view of the board's cards, not a separate epic hierarchy. **Burn-up**: work done and total scope as two lines per sprint and per project — in issues, or in points with estimation on (`GET /api/metrics/burnup?unit=issues`) — so scope growth is visible apart from velocity.
18. **Dependencies** (`#/graph`): cards laid out in layers by their longest dependency path (`dag.js`) with `dependsOn` edges; nodes are focusable, named elements (not inside an image), and the view is hidden when there are no edges.
19. **Intake and triage** (NEW-dashboard-10; v1 by DEC-51; FINDINGS PRC-01). A Stakeholder files an issue from the same *New issue* form and `c` key as a Member; the server files it through `issue.file` ([teams](teams.md) item 6), with the project lead as its assignee. A Backlog issue filed by a person below Member, by an integration or by an import, that no Member has triaged, is listed in the built-in **Triage** view (the View menu, query `is:untriaged`), oldest first. Each row offers four decisions, each one action with its key: **Accept into Backlog** (`1`; the issue stays in Backlog, now triaged — never Accept of finished work, and it records no `card/accepted`), **Decline** (`2`; *Won't do*, with a required reason), **Duplicate of** (`3`; an issue picker, then *Won't do* with the link) and **Snooze** (`H`; until a time, after which the row returns). Each records `issue/triaged {decision}` and the row leaves. Seshat's existing triage suggestions ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.18 item 2) show beside each row as proposals, applied only by a person. While Triage holds rows, the project lead's Inbox shows their count under *Needs you*. In Solo, where one person files everything, the view is hidden. No new state and no new permission.
20. **The sprint lifecycle** (NEW-dashboard-11; v1 by DEC-51; FINDINGS PRC-02). A sprint is *planned*, *active* or *completed* (stored `planned`, `active`, `closed`: the on-screen word is *completed*, [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.7 item 7), and a project has at most one active sprint. **Start sprint** (a Member, on a planned sprint: in its header, the Sprint menu and the palette) records the start with the committed issues, and their points when estimation is on. **Complete sprint** opens a sheet listing the done and not-done issues and asks where the not-done ones go — *the next planned sprint*, *a new sprint* or *Backlog* — then applies the moves and the close as one recorded group. A completed sprint has a **sprint report**, computed from the ledger: committed at start, added, removed, completed and carried over, as issues or as points with estimation on; it opens from the sheet, from the completed sprint's header and from the palette (*Sprint report*). When Seshat judges a sprint due to close it offers a `close_cycle` proposal that a person applies; Seshat never closes a sprint itself ([DEC-36](../DECISIONS.md#dec-36--the-ai-is-a-teammate-that-proposes-people-decide)). The proposal kind and its words are model-facing, so they follow [PROMPT_STANDARD](../PROMPT_STANDARD.md) (rule 18: a tool description of at most two sentences; rule 13: the words from Seshat's copy module; rule 35's steps before it ships); the tool `propose_close_cycle`, the proposal kind, the records `cycle/started` and `cycle/completed` and the report's computation are [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.7 item 7a's (NEW-planner-pm-13, PM-N13-1 to -6). When an action needs a sprint and none exists, the page offers **New sprint** and **Plan a sprint with Seshat**, and never shows an API path (the toast *create one with POST /api/cycles* is retired).
21. **Full-text search** (NEW-dashboard-12; v1 by DEC-51; FINDINGS PRC-04). Free words in the palette or the query box match title, key, description, acceptance criteria and comment text, across every project the person can see, Done and Won't do issues included. The palette lists matches under *Issues*, each with key, title, project, its column and the matched words in context; the query box filters the board and list as before. The index is SQLite FTS5 in the built-in `node:sqlite`, with no library (DEC-51, which settles DESIGN_GAPS b14's *FTS5 when the build has it, else a scan*: the build has it, as the reuse ranking already uses, [design-stage](design-stage.md) §4). The index is a projection rebuilt from the ledger: it respects visibility and access levels, and an erasure (`ledger/erased`) removes the erased text from it, so a search never returns erased text. On the W9 seeded board of 10,000 issues an answer comes within the interaction budget (p75 ≤ 200 ms, FINISH_LINE_PLAN §A).
22. **New issue by type** (NEW-dashboard-15; FINDINGS BRD-05). *New issue* offers the type, priority, labels, sprint and assignee as property pills, in DEC-31's type words. When the type is **Bug** it also asks *What happened*, *What you expected*, *Steps* and *Release* (the tagged releases), each optional; the planner uses them as the fix card's reproduction input ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.16). When a person tells Seshat that something is broken, Seshat asks for the same fields; that is a change to Seshat's model-facing words, so it follows [PROMPT_STANDARD](../PROMPT_STANDARD.md) rule 35 before it ships. Where the repository has `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE` issue forms, their fields are used for the matching type; their text is repository text, shown as labels and never sent to a model as instructions.
23. **Undo for field and bulk edits** (NEW-dashboard-16; FINDINGS BRD-07). An applied inline or bulk field edit, a move, a hold (*Put on hold*) or a *Won't do* shows a result toast offering **Undo** (`z`) for 10 s; `z` acts on the newest toast that offers it. Undo appends compensating events that restore the previous values — the log stays append-only — and says what it restored; a field another person changed since is left as it is and named (*Priority restored on 2 issues. CHR-9's priority was changed by Priya since, so it was left.*). Accept keeps its own grace window and revert ([review-git](review-git.md) S5; §2.5.10, §2.6) and never uses this undo.

### 2.5 Review

1. **Layout at 1440 px**: queue (296 px) · evidence (fluid, min 560 px, 24 px padding, 24 px between sections, 8 px heading to content) · facts rail (288 px); at 1024 px the rail becomes a *Facts* disclosure and the queue 248 px; on a phone the queue is a full screen; tapping a row opens evidence — the gates strip, failures and a *Changes* file list (tap a file for its diff) — with a fixed bottom bar of three 48 px buttons.
2. **Queue**: *Ready for review* (cards in Review, oldest first) and *Needs you* (parked cards, cards failed after the retry ladder, open decisions — the former Inbox —, slices at their appetite and proposed releases, longest wait first). Rows are 56 px (8/12 px padding): title (13/500, one line), then kind · state icon and short stop label · wait time (amber after 2 h) in 11 px secondary; a selected row has a 2 px `--text-primary` left bar on `--bg-overlay`. Each Review row shows the suggested accepters when the project has a `CODEOWNERS` file.
3. **Evidence column**, in order: breadcrumb and attempt selector (disabled when there is only one attempt); title; outcome line (*Passed on step 1 · 1.2s* or *Failed: Types, Tests · Looping on step 8*; *Built by Jane (person)* for a person-built card); **Reviewer findings** (§2.5.4a); the **gates strip**; **failures**; **changes**; *What the Worker tried* when the evidence carries abandoned hypotheses.
   - **4a. Reviewer findings**, attributed to *Reviewer* and its model id, titled with a count (*Reviewer · 1 unmet, 1 unclear*) and one line: *The Reviewer checked the diff against the card's criteria. It's advice, not a gate: Accept is yours.* Unmet findings first (fail icon and rule), then unclear (parked icon), then met, collapsed behind *n met*; each names its criterion and `file:line` (a link to the diff line) and can be acknowledged (`x`). Under them, the **coverage line** (*Reviewer read 3 of 3 files; 41 of 58 changed lines are cited by no finding*), with the uncited lines listed behind a disclosure. With no Reviewer, the unfilled-role reason ([review-git](review-git.md)); with no findings to show, the section is absent.
4. **Gates strip**: a segment per gate the evidence ran (32 px, 0 12 px padding, separated by 1 px hairlines, on `--bg-surface`) — icon, name, duration — in `gates.toml` order then derived gates (Size); failed segments get a 2 px `--state-fail` top rule and a count (*Types ✕ 3*); skipped ones say *Skipped because Types failed*. With **more than six gates**, gates are grouped by check family with a count (*Security 4/4 ✓*): failing groups first, worst first, then the rest in family order; a failing group names the gates that did not pass by their own names (*Gitleaks ✕ 2*) and holds its passed ones behind *+3 passed*, and its popover lists every gate in it (`gateStripModel`, DB-N1-1). A gate on a family-level rung (security, hygiene, robustness) is named by its id (*Gitleaks*, *Licenses*), so four security gates are never all called *Security*. On a phone (below 600 px) the strip is a vertical list, one gate or family a row, its text wrapping (DB-N1-2). Focus or hover shows a popover (at most 420 px wide) with the gate id and command in mono, exit code, the first three typed failures and *Show all*; click scrolls to that gate's failures; a passed gate opens its raw log once stored, and until then says *No output recorded for passed gates.* An empty gate contract (no `gates.toml`: `/api/gates` returns `empty: true`, [gates](gates.md) GT-T1-10) adds an amber **Gate contract empty** segment whose popover reads *No gates.toml: these results were not verified against a contract.*
5. **Failure block**, one per typed failure (8/12 px padding, 8 px apart): gate icon, `file:line:col` linked to the diff line, the code (`TS2353`); the exact error excerpt in mono; *Expected / Actual* when present; the repro command with Copy; *Suggested:*; a warning when the suggested file is a protected test (*The suggested file is a protected test. The fix belongs in the implementation.*). Identical messages group (*3 × TS2353 in tests/hasher.spec.ts*), expanding to show each line.
6. **Diff viewer**: files grouped *Implementation* (in scope, **ordered by risk**: files with failures, then files with unmet or unclear findings, then by changed lines — never alphabetically; each file gets a *seen* mark once it has been on screen), *Acceptance tests* (collapsed under a one-line summary of what they assert, lock icon, *staged by Sekhemet · not written by the Worker* (shown when the file is identical to its staged copy, `acceptance/<name>`); each test's approval state when the depth profile requires one — *Approved by Jane · example table*, *Needs approval* — and each **superseded** base test beside its new version, [planner-pm §2.16–2.17](planner-pm.md)), *Outside scope* (expanded, red rule) and *Other*; sticky 32 px file headers with a chevron, path, role, `+18 −0` and Copy path; old and new gutters (tabular, 40 px each, `--text-muted` as decoration); hunk headers in `--text-secondary` on `--bg-raised`; added and removed lines on tints with their `+`/`−` signs kept; inline annotation rows after the line a failure points at — a 2 px fail rule, gate icon and message, `role="note"` and focusable (`n/N`); unified or split (`u`); whitespace-only changes hidden with a toggle; files over 400 lines, lockfiles and generated files collapsed; a structural (difftastic) view when available.
7. **Screenshots**: for a visual gate, the baseline, the new capture and a pixel-difference overlay side by side (a slider between baseline and capture), and the images attached to the card; the pure pairing and pixel-diff logic is tested.
8. **Facts rail**: *Acceptance criteria* (DEC-52; *Done when* before it; the criteria; neutral bullets unless the Reviewer judged them; with every gate passing: *All checks passed. The criteria are checked by the acceptance tests.* only when the issue has acceptance tests, else *No acceptance test checks these criteria; the AI review read them against the diff.* (C2a, FINDINGS REV-01)); *Run* (steps, time, tokens, model, tool set, thinking policy and working method, built by); *Scope* (files in scope; protected tests); *Provenance* (gate contract hash, or *No gates.toml* with the same warning as the gates strip when the contract is empty, checkpoint sha, evidence id, each with Copy).
9. **Triage bar** (52 px, 0 24 px padding, 8 px button gap, `--bg-surface` with a 1 px top hairline, sticky under the evidence): **Accept `A`** (primary) is enabled only when the card is in Review, every blocking gate passed, triage is on, the ledger is intact, the person holds the Accept permission and may accept this card (independent accept, code owners — [review-git §2.4](review-git.md)), **every unmet or unclear finding is acknowledged and every Implementation file has been shown**; when disabled its reasons are written as a full-width checklist above the bar, one line each — GitHub's merge box — never only in a tooltip (*Accept needs every gate passing.*; *2 findings to acknowledge* / *1 file not yet shown*), with a refusal from the server first and the open review threads last (*1 open conversation*, which informs and blocks only on a project that requires resolved threads); on the issue page each line links to where it is met (C2a, FINDINGS REV-02, REV-03). On a phone the bar is equal 48 px buttons in one row, words only. **Request changes `R`** (secondary) and **Put on hold `P`** (ghost) (DEC-52; *Send back* and *Park* before it, kept as CLI aliases). A right-aligned hint shows `j k` · `Space` · `?`. For *Needs you* cards, Accept becomes **Retry with planner** once that endpoint exists; until then only Request changes and Put on hold. In the Team setup the bar adds **Comment** (ghost), a review comment with no verdict; an accept dismissed because new commits landed reads *Accept dismissed: new commits since it was accepted*; and on a project that requires resolved threads, Accept is disabled while one is open, naming it ([teams](teams.md) item 25, [review-git](review-git.md) §2.4).
10. **Accept**: `A` shows *Merging…* with a 3-second grace toast *Accepting "…"* `Undo Z`; `z` in the window cancels with no request sent. Then success *Merged to main as ba1338e* `Copy`; the line *You can revert it from the issue page.* joins it only once the issue page offers Revert (§2.6, NEW-dashboard-21), and until then the toast promises no revert. `Esc` in the window cancels as `z` does, with no request sent, and never leaves the page (leaving sends the Accept, REV-06). The row leaves and focus moves to the next; a refusal shows the server's message verbatim and the row stays.
11. **Request changes** (DEC-52; *Send back* internally and on the CLI): an inline composer above the triage bar (not a modal): a required textarea *What should the Worker do differently?*; **quick notes** generated from the failures and the unmet findings (clicking inserts); *Suggest as a playbook rule* (on), captioned *Your note becomes a candidate rule in Playbook when it names a file, symbol, gate or error.* ([review-git §2.4](review-git.md)); `⌘↵` sends, `Esc` cancels; then *Changes requested. The issue is back in To do with your note.* (board column words, DEC-52) and advance.
12. **Put on hold** (DEC-52; *Park* internally): a popover with an optional reason and presets *Waiting on me*, *Needs a decision*, *Not now*; `↵` confirms.
13. **States**: loading (a gates-strip skeleton of 4 boxes and 3 diff-line skeletons; the queue stays interactive); empty queue (a 24 px decorative glyph, *Nothing to review. Cards land here when every gate passes.* plus a count of Ready cards and a **Run them** button, not a command; the facts rail hidden); no evidence (**No attempts yet.** *Evidence appears after the Worker's first run. Budget: 40 steps.* — the card's own step budget —; triage shows only Put on hold); error inline with Retry; a card that changed state while open (*This card moved to In progress 3s ago.* `Reload`); offline and read-only per the shell.
14. **Decision request** (in *Needs you*; `decision.js`): the question (15/600) and its category; radio options, each with its label (13/500), consequence (13, secondary), *Effort +6 steps / ~7k tokens* and *Risk: …* in 11 px, and a *Preview* disclosure in mono (files, symbols, blast radius); the **Recommended** tag (neutral, not gold) with its rationale; the policy line (*If you don't answer by 16:00 (in 2h 14m), option B is applied.* or *If you don't answer, the card stays parked.* with a lock); the countdown updates each minute and turns amber under 15 minutes; destructive options have a red rule and an explicit confirm. An oracle disagreement ([planner-pm §2.17](planner-pm.md)) renders the same way, with the two sampled expected values as the options.
15. **Fast reviews.** A decision faster than 500 changed lines an hour is marked *fast review* in the ledger row and in Insights' review section; nothing is blocked ([review-git §2.2](review-git.md)).

### 2.6 Card view

**The issue page** (NEW-dashboard-8, DEC-34). `#/card/:id/{activity,checks,changes,ai_review,steps,plan}` (`1–6`, `ISSUE_TABS`; Activity when none is named, and the pre-B4.6 names still land: *evidence* on Checks, *thread* on Activity, *files* on Changes; tabs 32 px, the active one with a 2 px `--text-primary` underline — not gold, which is kept to its three places, §2.1.4). Header (96 px): breadcrumb (with *3 subtasks* when it has children), title with kind, state pill with its sentence, in the Team setup a **Watch** toggle and the avatars of who is viewing now (§2.13.8), triage buttons (same rules as Review) and a `⋯` menu (Copy id, Open worktree path, View in activity log (DEC-52), and the issue actions of the **Issue actions** bullet below). Who and what — assignee, delegate, reviewers, watchers and the rest — sit in the **properties rail** (below; DEC-51), not the header.
- **Above the tabs** (DB-N8-1, DB-N8-2; `issue_view.js`): the **Description** (the card's spec) and the **Acceptance criteria**, each with its id and its check state in words — *Passing* when every test gate of the attempt shown passed and the issue has acceptance tests staged (with none, *No test checks it*: passing tests prove no criterion — C2a, FINDINGS REV-01), *Failing* when a failing test names its id (a staged case names the criterion it proves, planner-pm PM-P1-17), *Not proven while the tests fail* otherwise, *Not checked yet* before any test gate ran — and *2 / 4* passing in the heading (`criteriaChecks`); the two fold into one disclosure. Below them the **agent bar**: the Agent with its *AI* badge, its state in DEC-34's words (*queued · working · needs you · paused · done*) and a sentence (*The agent is working on step 5 of 12.*), and the controls its state allows (`agentPanel`): while it works, **Pause** and **Take over** (Take over on a running agent asks for the pause first, since the server takes over only a stopped one); after Pause is asked, *Pausing at the end of step 5.*; paused, **Hand back** (with an optional note, inline) and **Take over**; taken over, **Run checks on my work** (`submit-take-over`) and **Hand back**; a run a person stopped (`human_abort`) is one state, *paused*, with *Stopped by Nora after step 2. Its work so far is kept; Resume continues from its last checkpoint.* — the person named from the ledger (*you* only in Solo, whose one person stopped it), never a fixed *you* — and **Resume** (the hand-back route, which takes a stopped issue from In progress or Verify back to To do; C2a, FINDINGS ISS-02, ISS-03). The server hands back only a card that stopped with `paused` (worker-loop WL-N10-2) or, as Resume, `human_abort`, so a take-over that did not start from a pause is refused with its reason in a toast until the loop can resume from a person's work (§4).
- **Activity** (DB-N8-1, DB-N8-3; `activity.js`, `activityItems`) — the issue's conversation, oldest first: moves in the board's words, the Agent's plan (its `note` calls, quoted), runs of plain steps as one row (*took steps 2–3 · last editing src/ledger.ts*, linked to Steps), each gate result (*Checks ran on step 4: Tests failed · 2 errors*), a pause or take-over, Seshat's review, answered decisions, the people's messages and hand-back notes with the step each reached (*Seen by the agent at step 5*, or *Not read yet*), and the Agent's open questions with their options as buttons, the default marked *(default)* and the line *Default: 64 zeros. The agent continues with it unless you answer.* for a safe default or *The agent waits for your answer.* otherwise (a destructive option takes a second press). *Every activity log entry* (DEC-52) adds the rest of the card's ledger in sentences (the former Thread tab). While the Agent works, a message box labelled *Message the agent — it reads this at its next step* (DB-N8-2; `POST /api/cards/:id/message`).
- **Checks** — the Review composition's gates strip, failures, screenshots and runs-outside-the-sandbox list, with the facts rail (`EvidencePane.checksHtml`).
- **Changes** (DB-N8-4; `changes.js`) — the files relevant to the card (path, role *In scope* / *Protected test* / *Outside scope* (DEC-52: *Files in scope*, formerly *May edit*), `+/−`, gate failures per file; a row brings its diff into view), then **Line comments**, then the diff grouped by role. Selecting a diff line fills in its file and line; a form (file, line, comment) reaches every line from the keyboard. A second comment on a line joins the first (`addLineComment`, validated by the server's own rule). *Request changes with 2 line comments* (DEC-52; in Review or On hold) opens the request-changes note, which lists them and sends them with the reason (`sendBackBody` → `POST /api/cards/:id/return { reason, comments }`), each an instruction to the next attempt (worker-loop WL-N10-4).
- **AI review** — Seshat's review of the diff (`card/review`), or *No AI review yet…*.
- **Steps** — one row per step: tool calls (`write_file src/hasher.ts`) with their observation summaries, gate results, tokens and time right-aligned; loop detection annotated where it fired; the final row states the stop. Live steps append from `card/step` events with no animation, and the running step's row (*Step 5 of 12 · the model is writing*) shows the model's output as it streams (`event: tokens`, the tail of the step so far), replaced by the step's summary when its `card/step` arrives; only the open Steps tab of a running card listens, so no other view keeps token text (NEW-dashboard-3, `live.ts`); auto-follow pauses when scrolled up (*3 new steps ↓*); `write_file` rows expand to the written content.
- **Plan** — spec; *Acceptance criteria* (DEC-52) with each criterion's id and approval; the requirements it traces to, with version and a *Needs re-checking* mark (DEC-52) when revised since; *Files in scope* (DEC-52) and protected *Acceptance tests* (as example tables where staged that way, with their approval state); budgets (steps, tokens, seconds as used/budget bars); difficulty as a 10-segment meter with routing (*Direct / Edit sketch / Split*) and the edit sketch when there is one; *Waits on* and *Unblocks*; the slice's rationale; the planner's repair plan for attempt 2+, read from the card's dossier ([worker-loop](worker-loop.md)).
- Tabs with no data say so (*No steps yet. The Worker hasn't started this card.*).
- **Waiting on approval** (planner-pm PM-N7-5): a card in Planning whose criteria no person has approved shows, above the tabs, *Waiting on your approval of its criteria*: each waiting card's criteria with their ids, its example rows (given → expected) and, by the depth profile, its example tables or test files, and one **Approve** button. Approve sends back the SHA-256 of what it showed (`POST /api/cards/:id/approve`, [PM_CONTRACT](../PM_CONTRACT.md)); a plan that changed since is refused and shown again. A Member's `issue.edit`.
- **Properties rail** (NEW-dashboard-19; DEC-51 adopts the approved Issue mockup's rail; FINDINGS ISS-01). A 288 px label/value list on the right, with no card around it (§2.13), in this order: *Assignee*, *Delegate* (with its *AI* badge and state), *Reviewers*, *Reporter* (who filed it, read from its creation on the ledger), *Type*, *Priority*, *Points* (only with estimation on), *Sprint*, *Epic*, *Release*, *Labels*, *Due*, *Blocked by* and *Blocks* (each issue with its column), *Branch* (mono, with Copy) and *Watchers*. Each value is edited in place with the list's editors (§2.4.15: the same menus, keys and events), and an applied edit offers *Undo* (§2.4.23); a field the viewer's level cannot change is read-only with the level note beside it (§2.15.7). From 1024 to 1279 px the rail is a *Properties* disclosure under the header; on a phone it is a section above the tabs.
- **Ready to start** (NEW-dashboard-14; FINDINGS PRC-12). On an issue in Backlog or To do (stored backlog, ready or planning) a *Ready to start* list above the tabs names each of [kernel](kernel.md)'s entry conditions for building, as *Met* or *Not met* with its reason (*Acceptance criteria not approved yet*). It reads the conditions the kernel enforces; it enforces nothing new and loosens no check.
- **Issue actions** (NEW-dashboard-21; v1 by DEC-51; FINDINGS ISS-04). The `⋯` menu, the peek and the palette offer the closing and reopening actions the CLI has, in NAMING's words: **Won't do** (an issue in Backlog, To do, In review or On hold; a reason is required; it moves to *Won't do*), **Reopen** (a *Won't do* issue; it moves to To do) and **Revert** (a Done issue). Revert first shows a confirmation naming what it does — *Revert adds a commit to main that undoes ba1338e, and moves this issue back to To do.* — and needs the project's Accept rule, as Accept does ([teams](teams.md) item 7). It adds a revert commit of the issue's squash and records `card/reverted` with both shas ([review-git](review-git.md) RG-S5-10); when git cannot apply the revert, the issue stays in Done and the server's reason is shown with the files it names. The server calls are `POST /api/cards/:id/{reject,reopen,revert}` (`reopen` is new, [runtime](runtime.md)). *Won't do* and *Reopen* offer Undo (§2.4.23); Revert is undone only by its own revert.
- **Criteria view** (NEW-dashboard-17; FINDINGS REV-10). When a person whose first-run route is *I manage the work* — in the Team setup, whose profile label is Product owner or Stakeholder ([teams](teams.md) item 8) — chooses *Review it* (Status's Needs you, §2.8.4, or the Inbox), the issue opens on its criteria view: each acceptance criterion in plain words with its check state, the AI review's summary, the visual check's screenshots, and **Accept** and **Request changes**, with the diff one tab away (Changes). Accept there records the same `card/accepted` under the same gates, Accept rule and enabling conditions as Review's triage bar (§2.5.9), the acknowledgement of findings and the showing of each Implementation file included; a condition not yet met is written beside Accept with a link to where it is met (§8 question 7).

### 2.7 Seshat: the panel and the full view

1. **Two places, one thread.** A right **panel** toggled with `⌘J` from any view: a 400 px dock from 1280 px (the view narrows; board columns relax to a 184 px minimum and the To do / In progress columns scroll beside pinned In review and On hold); a 380 px overlay from 1024 to 1279 px; hidden on `#/pm` and below 768 px (the bottom bar's PM tab opens the full view). Open or closed is remembered per browser. The **full view** `#/pm` has a 720 px reading column and a 288 px rail: *Open proposals*, *Worker* (state, step, paused), *What Seshat can see* (board snapshot time, last run, ledger head) and the three strongest *What Seshat has learned about you* statements, with *See all 4 and edit them in Playbook* and *Stays on this machine*.
2. **Header** (52 px): *Seshat · Project manager*, a plain monogram avatar (24 px rounded square on `--bg-overlay`, the letter *S* at 600 weight in `--text-primary`; no gold, face or gradient), and a **presence line** — *Replies in about a minute* when idle, the current phase with its timer when waiting (*Loading the project manager · 0:31*), so the state is visible when the thread is scrolled. **The chat panel names no model** — not in the header, its details or the cost line; Seshat's model is named on Configuration with every role's (owner decision O3, §2.16).
3. **Messages**: yours right-aligned on `--bg-raised` (max 85%) with the time in 11 px secondary; Seshat's full-width prose, with no bubble, under a one-line header (avatar, *Seshat*, time); tool use and research steps as quiet, collapsed detail under the reply, prose first; a question the Worker asked (its `ask`) that Seshat answered appears in the same thread, labelled as the Worker's (*The Worker asked about @hasher*), never as yours (attribution is [kernel](kernel.md)'s); system lines (*Seshat was restarted; the thread continues.*) as one centred 11 px secondary line. Markdown is limited to paragraphs, lists, bold, code, fenced code and `###` headings, rendered by an escape-first renderer; links from model text render as text.
4. **Card chips**: `@card` in either direction renders state icon, key and title (32 characters); click peeks, `⌘`-click opens; **unknown ids render as plain mono text**. `@` in the composer opens a card picker that fuzzy-matches titles and keys; `↵` inserts the chip. In an issue's comment box the same picker lists the project's members, then the AI teammates (*Agent*, *Seshat*, each with its *AI* badge), and mentioning someone who cannot see the project asks whether to invite them ([teams](teams.md) item 23). **Cites** render as *Based on:* chips for cards, runs (to `#/runs/<id>`) and evidence (to the card's Evidence tab); research sources render as a numbered **Sources** list (http and https only, new tab, `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, host in mono; a source with no URL is plain text).
5. **Context chip** above the composer: *Looking at: Board · Cycle 12 · 2 filters* or *Looking at: @hasher*; `✕` drops it for the next message; it returns when you move. Sent as `context: { cardId?, view }`. It exists because context sent silently feels like surveillance, and no context makes a person repeat themselves.
6. **Composer**: grows 1–8 lines from a 44 px minimum; **starter prompts** when the thread is empty or idle 12 h — *Standup*, *What's at risk this week?*, *Plan the next sprint*, **Start a new project** (which puts *Start a new project:* in the composer for the person to finish, with the line *Say in a sentence or two what you want built and who it is for. Seshat drafts a plan for you to review; nothing is created until you press Create project.* under it) — plus a contextual one (*Why did @hasher fail?* for a failed focused card; *Split @http* over 5 points). A **cost line** under the composer, in 11 px secondary, says in plain words what sending does: idle, *Seshat runs on this machine. Replies take about a minute.*; with the Agent working, *The Agent will pause after step 5 of 40 while Seshat answers (about 40s), then carry on.*; read-only: how to enable it (*This server is read-only, so Seshat can't be asked here. Whoever runs Sekhemet can restart it with sekhemet serve.*), and the composer is disabled. No API paths and no model name or id.
7. **Proposal group** at the end of a reply (`--bg-surface`, 1 px `--border-subtle`, radius 6; header 36 px; each proposal 12/16 px padding): header *Proposed changes · 3 open* with *Discard all* and *Apply all ⇧Y* (*Apply 3 changes to 5 cards*). A **field diff** per field (24 px rows): the label (secondary, 96 px), the before value struck on the fail tint, an arrow, the after value (primary, 500 weight) on the pass tint; values as people read them (priority glyph and word, `3 pts`, cycle and epic names, *Worker* / *You*, `Sep 29`, *None*); labels diff as a set (`+ security − later`). Create and split show numbered new cards with kind, points and *waits on 1*; moves show *Ready → Backlog*; reorders show *Position 7 → 2 in Ready*; park and unpark show the reason. States: *Open*; *Applied* (one line, pass check, *by you at 09:05*); *Discarded* (one struck line); *Stale* (the diff with an amber rule and *@hasher changed after Seshat proposed this. Ask again for a fresh proposal.*; Apply disabled, the reason stated inline). *Apply all* stops at the first failure (*Applied 2 of 3. "Split @http" failed: card is in progress.*). Applied is final and says so. **Imports use the same component** (*Import from Jira CSV · 42 proposed changes*), and so do edits to the exported project documents coming back as proposals ([design-stage §2.3](design-stage.md)).
8. **Waiting is a procedure with times**, not a spinner. A pending block under Seshat's header shows rows (24 px; 12 px icons; times 11 px, right-aligned, tabular) and a total timer since the message was queued:

| Phase | Row | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `waiting_for_step` | **Pausing the Worker after step 5** | *Waiting for step 5 to finish. The Worker is never stopped mid-edit.* |
| `loading_pm` | **Loading the project manager · about 40s** | A 2 px lapis bar toward the ETA (`etaSeconds`); past it the bar stops and the row reads *Taking longer than usual.* |
| `thinking` | **Thinking** | Elapsed; after 90 s *Long answers can take up to two minutes on this machine.* |
| `resuming_worker` | **Resuming the Worker** | *Reloading the Worker; step 6 starts next.* The reply is usually already shown above this row. |
| `idle` | (block removed) | — |

   Completed rows get a pass check and their duration; the current row a lapis ring and a running timer (tabular, updated once a second — the text changes, nothing moves) and `aria-busy="true"`; future rows an empty circle in secondary. Under the rows, once: *Only one model fits in memory, so the Worker waits at a safe step boundary and continues from step 6 once Seshat has replied. You can keep working; the reply lands here.* Worker rows are omitted when nothing was running. The shell bar and the Agent status line say *Worker paused after step 5 while Seshat replies* (lapis, not amber); the running tile reads *Paused for Seshat · step 5 of 40*. A second message shows *Queued · Seshat answers in order*. An error shows *Seshat couldn't reply.* with its cause in a plain sentence — never the exception's text, which goes to the server's log — and `Retry` (same text and context); when no model answers, a link to Configuration › Models (K3 item 12, FINDINGS PM-01). With no model, a status-shaped question (*How is it going?*) is answered with the standup from the Activity log, as `/status` is (PM-02). Offline freezes timers and disables the composer with *Offline. Your message would not reach Seshat.*
9. **Surface states**: **Seshat isn't on this server yet.** with the details line *This Sekhemet server has no project-manager endpoints (`GET /api/pm/thread` returned 404). Update Sekhemet and restart `sekhemet serve`.* (composer disabled with the reason; the nav item stays); first conversation — a one-paragraph local introduction (*I'm Seshat, the project manager for Chronicle. I read the board, the runs and the ledger, and I propose changes you approve. I never change the board myself.*) then the starters; loading (three skeleton lines); thread error (a 5xx): **Couldn't load the conversation.** *The server returned 500.* `Retry`.

### 2.8 Status: the project page for the stakeholder and the team

`#/status` (`g s`; the phone's first tab). One page, from the same data, for a stakeholder who is not a software engineer and for the people building the project ([DEC-37](../DECISIONS.md#dec-37--status-serves-the-stakeholder-and-the-team-from-the-same-data)). Top to bottom:
1. **Headline, health and the update.** A **sentence headline** in plain words from the ledger (*4 of 6 issues done. 1 needs a new plan; Seshat suggests splitting it.*) — facts only: the health word is a person's call, not the headline's. The project's **health** — *On track*, *At risk* or *Off track* — with the name of the person who set it and the date (*At risk · set by Priya · 24 Sep*), or, in the Team setup, *No health set* (in Solo health is optional and nothing is shown until one is set, [teams](teams.md) item 28); only the project lead or a Member who leads a release sets it, and never a model ([teams](teams.md) item 28). **Write update**: Seshat drafts the weekly project update in five parts — status, done, next, risks, asks — and the person edits it and presses *Post* ([teams](teams.md) item 29, [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.18); the latest posted update shows under the headline with its author and date; in the Team setup, 7 days since the last posted update (or since the release started, if none) show *Update missing* to the lead, and in Solo nothing is ever shown as missing ([teams](teams.md) item 29).
2. **Key numbers**, one strip: the **forecast** as a range — the 50% and 85% dates by Monte Carlo over issue throughput ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.6 item 3), with the target (*50% 14 Oct · 85% 21 Oct · target 18 Oct*), or *Not enough history yet* below the minimum, never a single date; **requirements done** per release ([PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH](../../research/PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH.md): done is computed from evidence and closed by a person, never claimed): *Release 1 · 9 of 11 requirements done* — its Must have requirements proven, the planner's count (`provenLine`), which Seshat's /status says in the same words (FINDINGS STA-01) —, with requirements whose tests pass but whose strength rule is unmet counted apart (*2 tests too weak*) and those that need re-checking named (DEC-52; *suspect* internally); **issues done**; the **sprint** (*Sprint 4 · 3 days left*, today counted, as the board's sprint header counts it); and **needs attention** (a count that links to Needs you). Under the strip, the appetite used, in plain words (*18 of 24 issues, 3 of 5 days*).
3. **Burn-up** for the current release, or the sprint: done and total scope as two lines, so scope growth shows apart from progress, with the forecast range as a band and the target date as a line (§2.4.17).
4. **Needs you**, each item with plain buttons (*Review it* — which opens the criteria view for a person who manages the work, §2.6 —, *Answer*, *Take off hold* (DEC-52; *Unpark* before it); for a slice at its appetite, Seshat's three choices *Accept as it is* (a proven release only), *Move the rest to Later* (every requirement that is not a Must have and not done; DEC-31's *Later*, never "nice-to-have"), *Extend* (only while nothing in it is unplanned; the server also asks for red tests, [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) PM-P13-9); for a proposed release, *Read the notes* and *Tag the release*; for tests awaiting approval, *Check the examples*; for a plan sent for approval, *Review plan*). Beside it, **Waiting on others**: questions, reviews and approvals waiting on someone else, each with its owner and how long it has waited.
5. **Requirements** by MoSCoW group — *Must have*, *Should have*, *Could have* ([DEC-31](../DECISIONS.md#dec-31)) — and state: *done*, *in progress*, *tests too weak*, *blocked*, *not started* (the on-screen words for §2.4.17's proven, planned, passing with strength unmet, and unplanned; a blocked requirement has an issue that is blocked). *Tests too weak* is never counted as done.
5a. **Next release** ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.15 item 8a, NEW-planner-pm-12; C2b): the issues accepted since the last tag outside a planned release, by title with their category; *Propose release* when it holds one (none otherwise), then the proposed release's notes and changelog with *Tag v…* (the Accept rule's people; disabled, saying so, for others); whether accepted work and tags are pushed to the project's remote, and each failed push with why ([review-git](review-git.md) §2.6 item 8).
6. **Risks** — the live signals that fired, each a sentence followed by *Suggested: … Why: …* ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.12, §2.18); applying a suggestion takes a person's press.
7. **Done this week** — each issue accepted, with who accepted it — and today's changes in plain words (the standup in plain mode, [planner-pm](planner-pm.md)).
8. **Who's working on what** — each person and the Agent, with the one item each is on now; no counts, rates or history per person.
9. **Models** — one line on the load of the models the project uses (*Coding model busy · 2 in queue, about 6 minutes*; [teams](teams.md) item 31).
10. **Flow** — one strip: median cycle time, 85th-percentile cycle time, throughput, issues sent back, and the share of issues whose checks passed first time; each links to Insights (§2.10).
10a. **Retrospective** ([planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.7 item 4, NEW-planner-pm-11; C2b): Seshat's draft when one is due — what went well, what slowed the team, proposed actions each with where a person applies it (*Open the Playbook*, *Open review capacity*, *File it in Triage*), its basis — with *Edit and post*; then the posted retrospectives, newest first, each named and dated.
11. An inline **Ask Seshat…** box and a primary **Start a new project** button that opens the start page with its live draft ([design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.11, NEW-design-stage-7; DEC-51 — before it, Seshat's panel with the start-project conversation); for a Stakeholder in the Team setup, or a Member who leads no project, it ends in *Send for approval*, not *Create project* ([design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.9; [teams](teams.md) item 21). It opens the start page in a workspace that already holds projects ([design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.11 item 6, DEC-57; D1 had it say how to start another server, under DEC-53 c3). Narrower than 768 px the button also sits at the top of the page ([design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.11 item 4).
12. **Never shown**, here, on Projects or in Insights: per-person velocity, leaderboards, rankings of the Agent against people, DORA numbers as targets, or a forecast as a single date (DEC-37).
13. Every label uses `plainStatus` (*Being planned*, *Being built*, *Waiting for review*, *Done*); no stop-reason codes, ids or gate names without words. It works at 400 px, in one column.
14. **Layout by width** (NEW-dashboard-18; DEC-51 adopts the approved Status mockup's grid; FINDINGS STA-08). In a window 1280 px or wider — the window's width, as every breakpoint in this spec (§2.2.2); the main area beside the sidebar is then about 1064 px, and 1200 px at 1440 — Status is the mockup's grid: the key-number strip first; then the burn-up (two thirds) beside *Needs you* and *Waiting on others* (one third); then a three-column band of Requirements, Risks and Who's working; then Next release (one third) beside the Retrospective (two thirds) (C2b); then Done this week and Models; then Flow. The headline and the update stay above the strip, and the reading order (for a screen reader and the keyboard) stays items 1–11's. In a window from 768 to 1279 px it is one reading column in items 1–11's order; narrower than 768 px it is one column (item 13).

### 2.9 The Learn layer

1. **Off for experts, on for learners**, set by the first-run question and toggled by a labelled **Tips** button (book icon and the word; DEC-31 names the layer *Tips* on screen) in the topbar, the account menu, Configuration › Preferences and the palette; kept per browser. It sets one `data-learn` attribute; **when off it adds no DOM and no layout**.
2. **Contextual, dismissible, findable again** (NN/g): when on, key terms get a dotted underline and a small *?* button — on column headers, the WIP count and bar, gate pips and the gates strip, the points field, the cycle header, each Insights chart, the story map's slices and requirement states, and Seshat's proposals (*I split this Path-first because…*). The *?* opens an **accessible popover** (keyboard reachable, not a `title`): two sentences on *what it is*, one line **from your own numbers** (*In review holds at most 3 because you review about 60 minutes a day and a review takes about 20*), and one link to the canon (Kanban Guide, INVEST, SPIDR — linked, not copied: CC BY-SA). Standard conventions are not explained.
3. **The Learn sheet**: 8–10 short lessons as a checklist — why each column exists and what moves a card; WIP limits and Little's law from their numbers; thin vertical slices (SPIDR) and INVEST; done = gates + a person's accept, and a project done = its chosen slices proven *and* accepted by a person ([planner-pm §2.15](planner-pm.md)); points versus time; cycles, burn-up and cumulative flow; work item age and the service-level expectation; why review is the bottleneck. **Show me** highlights the live element.
4. Content lives in one pure module (`learn.ts`), unit-tested and shared with Seshat's teaching mode. Empty states double as learning cues.
5. **Lessons for the practice a person performs** (NEW-dashboard-13; FINDINGS PRC-05). With Tips on, besides the board terms and flow numbers, `learn.ts` holds a lesson on each practice a person does here: reviewing a change; requesting changes with a useful note; approving acceptance criteria (at the criteria-approval hold, §2.6); priority and MoSCoW; blocked work and dependencies; the Definition of done (§2.16.5); triage (§2.4.19); sprint planning (§2.4.20); and the retrospective. Each lesson has the shape of item 2 — two sentences on what it is, one line from the project's own numbers (*You requested changes on 3 of your last 10 reviews; the notes that named a file were followed first time.*), and one canonical link (the Scrum Guide, Atlassian's or Linear's documentation), linked, never copied — in DEC-31's and DEC-52's words. The first time a lesson's subject appears on screen for a learner, the lesson is offered once, as the *?* of item 2 marked new; it never blocks the action. A lesson reaches Seshat's teaching mode only through Seshat's copy module and [PROMPT_STANDARD](../PROMPT_STANDARD.md) rule 35.

### 2.10 Insights
