`#/insights`, over 7, 30 or 90 days. Percentiles, never averages, because flow data is skewed and a mean hides the long tail people wait on.
1. **Headline**: cycle time (85th percentile), throughput (cards a day), work in progress (with how many are older than the 85th percentile), oldest in progress. The **service-level expectation** sentence: *85% of cards finish within 6.2 hours.*
2. **Four charts**, each titled as its question (a panel on `--bg-surface` with a 1 px hairline, 16 px padding, a 240 px plot): **Aging WIP** (*What is getting old?* — the columns To do → In review on the x axis and age on y, one dot per unfinished card, 50th and 85th percentile bands, dots above the 85th amber and labelled in words *Older than 85% of finished cards*; hovering or focusing a dot names the card, and clicking peeks it) first; **Cycle time** (*How long do cards take?* — scatter with dashed 50/85/95% lines labelled with their values (`50% 2.1h`, `85% 6.2h`, `95% 9.8h`)); **Throughput** (*How much finishes?* — daily bars with a 7-day moving average); **Cumulative flow** (*Where do queues form?* — stacked bands, Backlog at the bottom and Done at the top; running for In progress, parked for In review (the human queue), pass for Done, neutral surfaces for the rest; the legend ordered as the stack); plus **Burn-up** (§2.4.17).
3. **Worker capability** (`/api/capability`): per card kind, ordered by attempts (best-evidenced first), a point at the pass rate on a 0–100% track with its 95% Wilson interval as a bar and *16 of 18 passed · 89% (67–97%)*; fewer than 10 attempts says *Too few attempts to trust* in amber with a hollow point, and the caption counts those rows (*2 of 5 kinds have fewer than 10 attempts; treat those rates as rough*) and says a wide bar is uncertainty, not failure; pass rate by change size with an 80% line and the horizon sentence (*passes 80% of cards that change up to about 62 lines*), buckets with fewer than 10 attempts faded and labelled; the server's `note` verbatim under the section; retries run on an escalation model and person-built attempts are excluded and said so — *2 retries in the last run used the escalation model (…), not the Worker. They aren't counted in these rates.* — shown when a queue entry records that a retry ran on the escalation model (`escalated`, or a per-entry `model` that differs from the report's; `insights.js:351-357`). States: 404 *Worker capability isn't on this server yet* naming the endpoint; `sampleSize` 0 *No finished attempts yet*. The section renders independently of the flow metrics, so either can be missing.
4. **Stopping policy**, when a tuning report exists: the headline (*A 12-step cap would have cut 50.4 to 31 minutes (38% less) and kept 18 of 18 passes.*); a current-versus-recommended table (step budget, failed checks allowed, minutes, first-try passes, eventual passes); the copyable `sekhemet queue --max-turns 12`; a note when the failed-check limit also differs, because it has no flag yet; the caveat *Replay only stops a recorded run earlier than it really stopped. It never credits a pass the Worker didn't make, so it can't overstate what a tighter cap keeps. It can't tell you whether a looser cap would have rescued a failure.*; the full replay grid behind a disclosure. When the current policy is already best, it says so and offers no command.
5. **Review**: median review minutes per card with its basis (the 15-minute prior until five human reviews exist), review rate (changed lines an hour), and fast reviews counted apart.
6. Charts are SVG coloured only through token classes, tabular numerals, no gridlines beyond a baseline and percentile rules, each with a `figcaption` sentence and a hidden data table. Axis ticks are never repeated; plurals are correct. Under 3 finished cards: *Not enough finished cards to measure flow yet. Insights need at least 3; you have 1.* Flow metrics 404: *Flow metrics aren't on this server yet (`/api/metrics/flow` returned 404).*
7. **Model use** (`/api/metrics/flow`'s `modelUse`, [measurement](measurement.md) rule 4a): every model's tokens over the period, one row per model in the product's words — *Coding model* (its steps on issues), *Seshat*, *Planning model*, *Review model*, *Research model* — with its requests, tokens in, those reused from the cache, tokens out, of which thinking, and its share; the caption sums them (*Every model's tokens in the last 30 days: 200k in, of which 100k were reused from the cache, and 15k out, over 52 requests. …*). It shows even when too few issues finished for the flow charts; no model in the period: *No model ran in the last 7 days.*; a server without the figure: *This server doesn't count the models' tokens yet. Update Sekhemet and restart it.*

### 2.11 The other views

- **Runs** — a 240 px list of runs (date, time, model, *3 of 6 first try*, duration) and a scorecard: headline blocks *Passed first try*, *Passed after retry*, *Total time*, *Tokens*, each with a delta against the previous run (label 11 secondary, value 22/600 tabular, delta 11 in the state colour with an arrow icon); `Pass@1` appears only as a mono secondary label; a timeline whose segment widths sum to 100%, proportional to duration, filled by outcome and labelled by short id; the cards table (title, attempt, result, *Why it stopped*, steps, time, tokens, accepted sha; sortable); stops by reason, each linked; run settings (Worker, manager, model swaps, harness commit). A run in progress reads *Running · 2 of 6 cards · 4m* in its row, and the timeline grows live.
- **Activity log** (DEC-52: the on-screen name of the Ledger view, `#/ledger`; *ledger* stays the word in code and in documents about internals) — one sentence per event (*Planner created* **…**; *Worker moved* **…** *from In progress to Verify*; *Worker finished step 8 on* **…**), seq (tabular), time, actor, type (mono, `--text-secondary`) and the hash's first 8 characters in mono; filters by card, actor and type; a row opens the payload, payload hash, hash and prev; the header states integrity and when it was verified (*Activity log intact · 13 entries · verified 14:48:02*); a broken chain gives the first bad row a red left rule, and the header explains it; an erased field shows the erased marker and the `ledger/erased` entry that removed it ([kernel](kernel.md)).
- **Machine** — the memory gauge (6 px, ticks at the 85/90/94% thresholds and a sentence on what happens at each, owned by [models](models.md)): used / total GB, the level (*Normal*, *Warning*, *Critical*), swap in use; the active hardware tier; the models by role in a fixed order — Worker, Planner (Seshat), Reviewer, Researcher — each with its model id in mono (a link to that role on Configuration, where models are chosen), a state dot (*Resident*, *Swapped out*, *Not configured*), endpoint, keep-alive and a one-line description, with a note every role shares said once in the footer, and the memory model in one line (*One model is resident at a time on this machine; Sekhemet swaps them as the work needs (about 40 seconds each).*, or with `coResident` *This machine has room for all four at once, so nothing swaps.*); **telemetry sparklines** (120×24, a 1.5 px `--text-secondary` stroke with the last point as a state-coloured dot) of memory, decode speed and prefix-cache hit rate, sampled every 5 s while the page is open (`machine.js:160-205`); health checks, each a row with a 16 px icon, name (13/500), detail (12.5, secondary) and a plain fix hint (*No skills folder. Create `.sekhemet/skills/` to load skills.*); sandbox mode; worktrees (their count and card ids). **Reserve now** / **Release**: the machine's reservation in words (*Reserved by you since 14:02: nothing runs unattended until you release it*, *Your reserved hours, until 18:00*, or *Free: the overnight window is open*) and one button that reserves the machine now or releases it — the same implementation as `sekhemet dev reserve` and `release` ([surface](surface.md) item 20a), recorded with the person's principal, and also in the palette (*Reserve the machine*, *Release the machine*). While reserved, no backlog card starts unattended unless it is urgent and the overnight benchmark neither starts nor continues ([models](models.md) rules 20 and 20b); Release never changes `reserved_hours`. *Re-run checks* bypasses the 15 s cache (`?fresh=1`). A roster 404 shows *The model roster isn't on this server yet*, naming the endpoint.
- **Playbook** — a lede, once: *Learned from gate results and what you do, never from a model grading itself. Everything stays on this machine and is recorded on the ledger. A rule takes effect only after you approve it.* Then *Needs your approval* (newest first; **Approve** primary, **Edit**, **Retire**), *Active* (rules whose paired credit is falling first, with the sentence that says how they compare with comparable cards without them; rules retired automatically by [DEC-28](../DECISIONS.md#dec-28--one-rule-for-admitting-what-the-system-learns)'s test — at the fixed looks after 20, 40 and 80 pairs, when a one-sided exact test on the discordant pairs shows harm at 0.05/3 — move to *Retired* with that sentence; the rest by value), *Retired* (collapsed). Each rule shows who it is for (*For the Worker* / *For Seshat*), its source in words (*From your send-back note*, *From a fix that took the Worker several tries*, *From Seshat's end-of-run review*, *Seeded with the project*) and its age, scope chips (*Kind: Rules*, *Files: `src/**/hash*.ts`*, *Error: `TS2353`*, or *Applies to: every card*), a signed value bar relative to the page's largest |value| (red when negative), helpful and harmful counts with their icons, a reach chip (*This project* / *All projects*, the latter drawn slightly stronger), and evidence as `@card` chips with the quoted note (the first two shown, the rest behind *n more signals*). Edit is inline (a textarea, `⌘↵` saves, `Esc` cancels). Approve opens a reach picker, *This project* (1) or *All projects* (2), whose footer says *All-projects rules live in ~/.sekhemet and apply to every repository on this machine.* Every action is optimistic, reverts on failure and states the result (*Approved. The rule is given to the Worker from the next matching card.*). Seeded rules from `.sekhemet/playbook.toml` stay visible as active, read-only rules (*Edit in playbook.toml*) when the learning store does not carry them. When `/api/learning` returns 404, the page keeps the seeded rules and send-back suggestions from `/api/playbook` under a banner naming the endpoint. **What Seshat has learned about you** (`#/playbook/profile`): a lock line (*These stay on this machine, in the project's ledger. Edit a statement to correct it; dismiss it and Seshat stops using it.*); active statements grouped by category (Code style, Planning, Communication, Priorities), strongest first, each with a strength bar and word (*Strong* ≥ 0.7, *Moderate* ≥ 0.4, *Weak*), its source, its evidence with dates, **Edit** and **Dismiss**; dismissed statements collapsed at the end.
- **Projects** (`#/projects`, `g w`; `#/workspace` opens it) — the workspace's home, replacing the Workspace rollup ([teams](teams.md) item 5): a totals strip (*Waiting on you*, *Active projects*, *Shipped this month*, *Agent time today*); a table with one row per project the person can see — health (with who set it), the current release and its progress, the forecast range and the target, what waits on you, what the Agent is doing, and the lead; the **Waiting on you** list across projects, with wait times (the old rollup's list); and **Models on this server**, each with its load (slots in use, queue length) and the memory in use (the old rollup's machine load). A **New project** button (primary, top right) opens the start page with its live draft ([design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.11; DEC-51 — Seshat's start-project conversation before it; for a Stakeholder, or a Member who leads no project, it ends in *Send for approval*), whatever projects the workspace already holds, and offers *Add an existing repository* beside it (DEC-57, NEW-dashboard-26; D1 had it say how to start another server, under DEC-53 c3). With no projects the page shows one empty state — *Start your first project*, with a button that opens the start page — and no totals, table or empty columns. In Solo it lists the workspace's projects. A project added beyond the active-project cap shows *Paused* with the reason, *3 projects are active; pause one to resume this* ([runtime](runtime.md) item 4b). No per-person counts (§2.8.12).
- **Configuration** — models, the benchmark, review capacity, browser preferences and the project's configuration: §2.16. It replaces the Registry and Settings views.
- **Integrations** — renders what `/api/integrations` returns ([integrations](integrations.md) owns the catalogue and behaviour), **grouped by the `tier` field the API returns** (*Now*, *Next*, *Later*); nothing is hard-coded in the page, and nothing is connected by default. Under the lede the page says **where secrets typed here are kept** (`GET /api/integrations/secret-store`, `secretStoreNotice`): the OS store by name; on a host with none, that secrets are not saved and, until the person chooses, a *Keep secrets in a private file on this machine* button (security item 35, SEC-27c); each credential form repeats where its secret goes. *Now* cards have their controls; *Next* and *Later* cards are quiet — name, one sentence, *Planned*, **no button** — and still state the data they would send. Copy is keyed on the integration id; an id the page does not know renders from the server's `name` and `detail`. Every card states in one line **what leaves this machine when connected**:

| Card | Body and controls | Leaves this machine |
| --- | --- | --- |
| GitHub Issues and Projects | *Connected to acme/chronicle via gh* or *gh isn't signed in*; *Last synced 12 minutes ago*; last result *4 created · 9 updated · 2 skipped · 0 errors*; **Pull**, **Push**, **Sync both** (primary), with *Syncing with GitHub…* while one runs; errors verbatim | Card titles, specs, priority, points, cycle and state, to the repository you choose; uses your `gh` login, no stored token |
| Pull request on Accept | A named switch with the current behaviour beside it (*Accept merges locally as one commit*) | The card branch, its diff and gate results |
| Jira · Linear | **Export** (their CSV columns) and **Import…**; never shown as "Connected" | Nothing: you upload the file yourself |
| Slack | Webhook field, **Connect**, **Send test message**, **Disconnect**; the channel host, the last message and whether it was delivered; a lock line: the webhook URL is a credential kept in the user directory `~/.sekhemet` ([surface](surface.md) item 22) with mode 0600, never in the repository or the ledger | Standup text, titles of cards that need you, run summaries |
| Research web access | A switch; which search provider is configured, or how to configure one (*Web search needs a provider you configure: a self-hosted SearXNG, or a Brave or Tavily key in the environment; papers, page reads and GitHub work without one.*) | Search queries and the URLs of pages read; private and local addresses are never fetched |

  **Import** opens a sheet (format picker: Jira CSV, Linear CSV, GitHub JSON, Sekhemet JSON; file chooser; paste box); **Preview** renders the returned proposals with the §2.7 component and posts the same preview into Seshat's thread, and the sheet says so (*The preview is also in Seshat's thread.*). Export downloads `sekhemet-<project>-<format>.csv|json`. A 404 shows *Integrations aren't on this server yet* with the endpoint in a details line, and no catalogue.

### 2.12 Voice and vocabulary

1. **One label source.** Every column, state, stop reason, gate, kind and actor label comes from `vocabulary.ts` (and `plainStatus` for the plain mode), served to the page; the browser never re-derives a label, and no second status map exists anywhere. Words follow [NAMING](../NAMING.md). A stop reason the vocabulary does not know falls back to its humanised enum, never to a blank.
2. Internal enums appear only in mono where a developer might grep for them (evidence detail, ledger rows). Compiler and gate errors are quoted verbatim. Actors are named *Agent*, *Planning model*, *AI review* (the Reviewer's findings), *Research model*, *You* (or the person's name) and *Sekhemet* (DEC-31).
3. **No operator language on user surfaces**: no API paths, model ids in running copy (Machine and Configuration name models; the chat panel names none, §2.7), or "N-step budget" on the board face. A missing endpoint reads *X isn't on this server yet* with the endpoint in a details line.
4. **The essentials the label source must carry** (short label · sentence · tone):

| Kind of term | Labels |
| --- | --- |
| Stop reasons | Passed · *All checks passed on step N.* · pass — Passed after a Planning model retry (`passAfterEscalation`) · pass — Out of steps · *Used all N budgeted steps without passing.* · fail — Looping · *Repeated the same actions without changing any file.* · fail — Stalled · *No file changed for 3 steps.* · fail — Couldn't fix · *Tried N repairs; the same check kept failing.* · fail — **Paused for memory** · *Stopped safely at 94% memory. Resumable.* · **parked, not fail** (a safety stop) — Paused for quota · *The model provider's limit was reached.* · parked — Harness error · *Sekhemet failed, not the agent. See the ledger entry.* · fail — Out of scope · *Tried to edit a file this card may not touch.* · fail — Too hard for this model · *Needs a split or a stronger model.* · parked — Stopped · *A person stopped this attempt. Its work is kept; it resumes from its last checkpoint.* · neutral (no fixed *you*: every viewer reads it; the issue page names the person from the ledger — C2a, FINDINGS ISS-03) |
| Checks (gates, internally) | Parse · Types · Tests · Lint · Size · Visual (id kept in mono in evidence; Types shows its command); states Passed · Failed · **Skipped** (configured, not run because an earlier blocking check failed) · **Not run** (the card never reached its checks) · Running; *4 of 4 checks passed*, *All checks passed*; Size's sentence *2 files, +11 −0 (limit 3 files, 200 lines)* |
| Issue type (kind, change and split) | DEC-31 supersedes the invented kind labels: a card shows one **issue type** — *Story* · *Task* · *Bug* · *Spike* · *Epic* — from `issueTypeOf` (the stored `kind`, `change` and tier; [NAMING](../NAMING.md#card-kind-change-and-split)), each with a one-line description in its popover. The stored `kind` stays internal (tool sets, rule scopes, the capability partition) and appears only in mono, where a developer greps for it (a Playbook rule's scope). With Tips on, a split child names its SPIDR axis from its stored `split` (Spike, Path, Interface — the *user* interface —, Data, Rules), never derived from its kind ([planner-pm §2.2](planner-pm.md)) |
| Requirement states | Done (proven on main) · Tests too weak (DEC-52; *Passing, strength unmet* before it) · Planned · Unplanned · Needs re-checking (DEC-52; *Suspect* before it) · Cut; a release reads *Release 1 · 5 of 11 requirements done*; priorities Must have · Should have · Could have (MoSCoW), *Later* for what is out of the release |
| Card facts | *Acceptance criteria* (DEC-52; *Done when* before it) · *Files in scope* (DEC-52; *May edit* before it) · *Acceptance tests* (protected) · *Waits on* / *Unblocks* · *Difficulty 6/10* · *Why it stopped* · *Checkpoints* · *Checks configuration* · *8 of 40 steps* (on the issue, never the tile) (a step is one model request and the tool calls it makes; an attempt holds one or more samples of steps, [NAMING](../NAMING.md#the-run)) · *Tokens 16.8k in · 1.4k out* · *Built by* |
| Actions | Accept · *Merges to main as one commit.* — Request changes · *Returns the issue to To do with your note for the agent.* — Put on hold · *Sets the issue aside. Nothing runs until you take it off hold.* — Take off hold — Won't do · *Closes the issue without building it. You can reopen it.* — Reopen — Revert · *Adds a commit that undoes the accepted change.* (DEC-52's words for Send back, Park and Unpark; Won't do, Reopen and Revert, NEW-dashboard-21) |
| Errors | What, why, action: *Couldn't load evidence for hasher. The server returned 500.* `Retry`; a refused accept is titled **Couldn't accept.** and quotes the server, whose text names the board's columns, never stored states (*This issue is In progress, not In review.*; DEC-52); an empty send-back note is blocked client-side with *Add a note for the agent. It's what it reads next.*; *This action must come from the dashboard. Reload the page.* |

5. **The professional words** (NEW-dashboard-23; [DEC-52](../DECISIONS.md#dec-52--the-professional-words-naming-amended), which amends [NAMING](../NAMING.md) and DEC-31's table; the later ruling wins where they differ). On screen: the responsible person is the **Assignee** (stored `owner`), and the AI teammate or person who builds the issue its **Delegate**; *Send back* is **Request changes**; the ledger, the event log and the audit trail are the **Activity log**; *Park* and *Unpark* are **Put on hold** and **Take off hold** (the stored state stays `parked`; the column is *On hold*); *Suspect* is **Needs re-checking**; *Passing, strength unmet* is **Tests too weak**; *Done when* is **Acceptance criteria**; *May edit* is **Files in scope**. Error and refusal text names the board's columns (*In review*, *To do*, *Done*), never stored state ids. The code keeps its internal names, the CLI keeps the old verbs as aliases, `ledger` stays the word in documents about internals, and Pipeline stages (§2.4.1) still names the stored states for operators. Model-facing text is not renamed here: a change to it follows [PROMPT_STANDARD](../PROMPT_STANDARD.md) rule 35. The Admin's page of §2.17.5 keeps the name *Audit log* (§8 question 8).

### 2.13 Visual system

**The professional standard** (owner, 2026-09-25, from the mockup review). The dashboard reads like Linear, Jira or GitHub, never like a chatbot or a template:
- Activity is a timeline — system events as one compact line with a small icon, comments as a name, a time and plain text — never chat bubbles.
- One level of container: a page, a sidebar, a list or a table; no boxes inside boxes. Properties sit in a label/value list without cards.
- Real icons (inline stroke SVG, one set, one weight), never text glyphs (✓ ✕ × ···) or emoji.
- Copy is terse: a label or a short fact, never an explanatory sentence where a label will do; explanations belong in Tips.
- One primary action per view (the dark ink button, item 7; DEC-51); everything else is a secondary button or a link. The brand gold marks only the active item, the focus ring and the logo's sun disc (§2.1.4).
- The vocabulary is DEC-31's.

1. **Tokens** are the single source of truth for colour, published as CSS custom properties and JSON (`tokens.ts`, `/tokens.css`, `/tokens.json`); components never hard-code a colour. Sixteen roles per theme — `bg-base`, `bg-surface`, `bg-raised`, `bg-overlay` (the surface ladder: depth by luminance, **no drop shadows**), `border-subtle`, `border-strong`, `border-control` (P12), `text-primary`, `text-secondary`, `text-muted`, `accent` (Egyptian gold), `state-pass` (Nile green), `state-fail` (red ochre), `state-running` (lapis), `state-parked` (needs you), `state-blocked` — plus derived `on-accent`, `on-ink` (text on a primary button, DEC-51), `on-state`, `scrim`, `tint-{pass,fail,running,parked}` (12% Basalt, 10% Sand) and layout constants. The values are in `tokens.ts`, not here; the contrast test (§2.14.1) is what holds them.
2. **Colour roles (P12):** the warning/parked hue is moved clearly apart from the accent (a copper: the mockups' `#C8743A` / `#9A4F1C` measured only 16° from the gold, so the copper sits 23° from the gold); **the copper keeps its hue, 7–10° from the red ochre of a failure, and is told apart from it by glyph and by lightness, never by hue alone** (lead decision under [DEC-42](../DECISIONS.md#dec-42--the-owner-delegates-the-outstanding-decisions-to-the-lead), B4.2): parked carries the pause glyph and fail the cross (`STATE_GLYPHS`, `vocabulary.ts`), and parked sits **at least 10 L\* further from the ground than fail** — lighter on Basalt, darker on Sand (`tokens.ts` holds `#DD8967` Basalt / `#743217` Sand; the earlier `#D46A3F` / `#9E4420` measured 1.8 and 0.3 L\* from fail). **The warning hue is its own** (DEC-51): what the copy calls *amber* — a wait past its limit, a sprint behind its pace, a capacity bar at its limit — is drawn in `--state-parked`, never in `--accent`, so "needs attention" never looks like the brand or a primary action; with primary buttons in ink (item 7), gold marks no action at all. **Disabled buttons use a neutral fill**, never faded gold; a **`--border-control`** role at ≥ 3:1 edges every input; **`--text-muted` is decorative only** (line numbers, disabled controls, decorative glyphs) and never carries text a person must read, placeholders included.
3. **Colour per tone.** A rule is 2 px — on the left of a tile, row or block, or on top of a gates segment. The running, failed-and-retrying and On hold tiles carry one in their tone. Running — fill, rule, text only on base/surface, tint for the live step; pass — quiet, no rule; fail — fill, rule, tint for failure blocks and removals, and the blocker flag (§2.4.4); parked — fill, rule, shell bars; blocked — the dependency link icon and secondary text for *waits on* detail.
4. **Type**: Inter and JetBrains Mono with system fallbacks that must look right on their own; sizes 11 / 12.5 / 13 / 15 / 18 / 22 px; leading 1.25 / 1.45 / 1.55 (code); tabular numerals for every number. Usage: wordmark 15/600, tracking −0.01em; view titles 15/600; card title in Review and the card view 18/600; scorecard numbers 22/600; section headings 12.5/600; body 13/400 and tile titles 13/500; meta, status lines and column counts 11 secondary; mono ids, hashes and event types 11 mono secondary; code 12.5 mono; `kbd` 11 mono in a 1 px `--border-subtle` outline, 3 px radius, 0 4 px padding, secondary; diff line numbers 11 mono in `--text-muted`.
5. **Spacing** 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32 px; **radius** 4 px controls and chips, 6 px cards, 0 for full-bleed panels; **motion** 120 ms ease-out, only for hover, press, focus, drawer and palette open (opacity plus an 8 px translate) and popovers; board reflow, streaming, patches and toast-stack shifts append with no animation; the running dot's pulse (1 → 0.35 opacity, 1.6 s) is the only perpetual animation; `prefers-reduced-motion` removes it and all translates, and drawers switch instantly.
6. **Icons**: one 1.5 px line set on a 24 viewBox, `currentColor`, round caps and joins, no fills except `dot`, no emoji or Unicode check marks in the product; distinct glyphs for every nav item (Review and Inbox never share one). **Brand mark**: a temple gateway — two tapered pylons with a sun disc on the horizon between them, drawn as the approved `Logo.dc.html` board draws it (DEC-51; FINDINGS VIS-02: the built mark put the disc inside the gate): on a 24-unit square, each pylon runs from the horizon at y 6.5 down to the base at y 21, its outer edge tapering (x 4.4 to 2.2 on the left, 19.6 to 21.8 on the right) and its inner edge upright (x 9.3 and 14.7), and the disc, of radius 2.4, is centred at (12, 6.5) — on the horizon the pylons' tops make, between them, never lower in the gate; the favicon and the app icon use the same geometry; the pylons in ink — the theme's `--text-primary` (light `#1C1A16`, one step from the `#1D1B17` first drawn, so the mark never needs a colour of its own / dark `#EDE7DA`) — the disc in gold, the theme's `--accent` (light `#8E6512` / dark `#C8952A`). The **app icon** and favicon: an ink tile with cream pylons (`#F3EEE3`) and a gold disc (`#D9B45A`). Minimum size 16 px for the mark and 80 px for the lockup; clear space one pylon width on every side; never recoloured, never on gold. It replaced the 1.5 px line glyph drawn in accent (B4.7). Wordmark *Sekhemet* in Inter 600, tracking −0.01em, `--text-primary`, 8 px right of an 18 px mark; no mascot, lion or tagline in the chrome. Where the product introduces itself: *Sekhemet — a coding harness for professional teams*.
7. **Component sizes**: sidebar item 28 px (8 px horizontal padding, 2 px gap; 16 px between groups; the bottom block 12 px padding); topbar 44 px; column header 36 px; tile padding 8/12 px; review queue row 56 px; evidence padding 24 px; gates segment 32 px; failure block 8/12 px; diff file header 32 px, line 20 px; triage bar 52 px; **buttons** 32 px high, 4 px radius, 12 px horizontal padding, with the `kbd` hint inside in 11 px mono — **primary in dark ink: a `--text-primary` fill with `--on-ink` text** (a derived role, the theme's ground: near-white on Sand, near-black on Basalt; DEC-51 — `--accent` with `--on-accent` before it), secondary on `--bg-raised` with primary text and a `--border-subtle` edge, ghost with no fill and `--text-secondary` text; palette 600 px wide, rows 36 px, group labels 28 px; toasts 360 px, bottom-left 16 px from the edges, stacking upward, 12/16 px padding, `--bg-overlay` with a `--border-strong` edge, a state icon, one line and an action, at most 3, success 4 s (`role="status"`), errors until dismissed (`role="alert"`), hover pauses; Seshat panel 400 px (header 52 px, 16 px padding, 24 px message gap, composer at least 44 px); proposal group header 36 px, diff rows 24 px; pending-block rows 24 px; view bar 40 px; cycle header 56 px; lane header 32 px; list group header 32 px; table row 36 px; cheat sheet a 720 px modal; bulk bar 48 px; chart plot 240 px; memory gauge 6 px; sparkline 120×24.
8. **AI identities and people.** Seshat and the Agent carry an **AI badge** wherever their names appear — the delegate chip on a tile, pickers (under *AI teammates*, never *Members*), Activity, the Inbox, Audit: the letters *AI* in 11 px at 600 weight, `--text-secondary`, in a 1 px `--border-subtle` outline with a 3 px radius, 4 px after the name ([teams](teams.md) item 18). The Agent never has an avatar (a role never does, [NAMING](../NAMING.md)); Seshat keeps its plain monogram (§2.7.2); a person never carries the badge. **Presence avatars** ([teams](teams.md) item 26): 20 px initials monograms of the people viewing an issue, overlapping by 4 px with a 2 px `--bg-surface` ring, at most three then *+n*; a card someone is editing or dragging shows that person's avatar at its top-right corner; a member active in the last 5 minutes has a 6 px `--state-pass` dot on their avatar. Presence is never an AI identity and never recorded.

### 2.14 Accessibility

1. Every colour pair used as text or as a control's only edge meets WCAG 2.2 AA **as used**, asserted by a test over the tokens in their actual pairings (placeholders, Sand accent on raised, state text on tiles): text ≥ 4.5:1, with body text (`--text-primary` on base and surface) ≥ 7:1; controls' edges, state icons and the accent ≥ 3:1. State-coloured text sits only on base or surface; on raised tiles state is carried by the icon (≥ 3:1) with secondary words.
2. **Nothing a person needs is only on hover**: WIP reasons, full status lines, gate names and priority meanings are in keyboard-reachable popovers or visible text. A `title` is only ever a pointer duplicate: its words are also visible, the element's accessible description (`tip()`, `dom.js`), or a keyboard and touch disclosure (a column's WIP reason is the first line of its options menu).
3. Landmarks (`nav`, `header`, `main`, `aside`); each board column a `group` labelled by its header, holding a vertical `listbox` of `option` tiles with roving tabindex, `aria-selected` and `aria-describedby` pointing at the status line; the list a real `table` with `aria-sort` on the sorted header and `aria-selected` on rows; the gates strip a list of named buttons (*Types: failed, 3 errors, 0.7 seconds*); diff annotations `role="note"` and focusable; WAI-ARIA tabs; palette and cheat sheet as modal dialogs that trap and return focus (the palette a combobox); the Seshat panel a `complementary` landmark labelled *Seshat, project manager*, with its thread as `role="log"`, announcing only final replies (*Seshat replied. 2 proposed changes.*), and the pending block's current row `aria-busy="true"`; proposals a list of labelled groups with Apply/Discard names including the summary; field diffs as `dl`; the bulk bar a toolbar announcing *3 selected*; toasts `role="status"` or `role="alert"`; one polite live region for the focused or selected card and triage results; background changes are not announced.
4. Focus order in Review: queue → Reviewer findings → gates strip → failures → diff files → triage bar; `Tab` inside the diff visits file headers and annotations, not lines.
5. Focus: a 2 px accent outline, offset 1 px, on `:focus-visible` (≥ 3:1 on every surface). Targets ≥ 24×24 px on desktop (copy buttons included) and ≥ 44×44 px on a phone (one query below 768 px, `base.css`; desktop density is unchanged). Layouts hold at 200% zoom. Every control has an accessible name.

### 2.15 How it is built

1. **Static, build-free ES modules** served from `packages/ui/web` under `/app/`; no framework, no CDN, works air-gapped; the served page is a small shell. Presentation logic is pure TypeScript in `packages/ui/src` (vocabulary, board model, tile model, nav model, keymap, learn content, filter language, flow maths), served as `/app/lib/*.js` and unit-tested with exact values; web modules only render. How `/app/*` is served safely (no `..` traversal, correct MIME types) is [runtime](runtime.md)'s.
2. Live data arrives on the server-sent event stream (`/api/stream`: `append` for ledger events including `card/step`, `pm` for PM status, `machine` every 5 s, `tokens` for the running model's output); windowing is the page's own (`virtual.js`); CSS is organised per component. The server enriches each card with a `display` object from the vocabulary (title, kinds, key, state label, status line, stop label, tone, entered-column time, waits-on, evidence summary) and publishes the vocabulary as `/vocab.json` for plugin panels.
3. **Responsive.** 1440 px: everything at full size. 1024–1279 px: labelled narrow sidebar; board columns min 220 px, scrolling beside In review and On hold in their own pane where they do not all fit (§2.4.2); Review's facts rail as a disclosure. < 768 px: the bottom bar; the board as one column with a segmented column switcher (*To do 4 · In progress 1 · In review 1 …*) and full-width tiles; the card view shows Evidence only; triage is one tap on 48 px buttons with send-back in a bottom sheet; no create or bulk actions; Status and PM fully usable.
4. **Palette.** 600 px at 12vh, `--bg-raised` with a 1 px `--border-strong` edge and no shadow, over `--scrim`; a 48 px, 15 px input; groups *Actions on the focused card* (Accept, Request changes, Put on hold (DEC-52), Won't do, Reopen, Revert (NEW-dashboard-21), Open, Copy id), *Cards* (title, kind, state icon), *Go to* (with chords), *Preferences* (theme, density, Learn), and *Seshat* — first, *Start a new project* for a query made of its words ("new project", "start"), and *Ask Seshat: <query>* when nothing else matches, which sends the query with what the person is looking at and opens Seshat (DB-P5-4); matched characters in `--text-primary` against the rest in `--text-secondary`; each row shows its shortcut; `⌘↵` opens a card in the card view; the empty query shows recent cards and top actions.
5. **Verification of every UI change**: seed a fixture project, open it at 1440×900, 1100×800 and 400×812 in both themes, compare with `docs/design/mockups/`, exercise the change's keys with the keyboard only, and require an empty error console; check each view against a contract-shaped fixture server **and** a server with the endpoints absent, so every "not on this server yet" state is seen.
6. **Security, page side (S3c):** every mutating request carries the per-session token the server issued to this page (not a constant header); the page runs under a Content-Security-Policy with no inline script and `frame-ancestors 'none'`; model and repository text reaches the DOM only through escaping renderers. The server side (Host check, token issue, headers) is [runtime](runtime.md) and [security](security.md).
7. **Team setup, page side** ([DEC-35](../DECISIONS.md#dec-35--one-product-two-setups-solo-and-team); replaces the company-server wording of D7): the account menu shows who you are (§2.2.6; superseded: "the footer shows who you are"); a control a person's access level does not allow is disabled, with the level they hold and who can do it written beside it (*You're a Stakeholder on Chronicle. A Member can start the Agent on this issue.*, [teams](teams.md) item 9); Accept is disabled with *This project's Accept rule doesn't include you* when the project's Accept rule ([teams](teams.md) item 7) does not name you, and, on a project where two or more people hold the Accept permission, with *You built this card; another person must accept it* or *You delegated this card to the Worker; another person must accept it*, naming who may ([review-git §2.4.1](review-git.md)) — the delegator being whoever recorded the latest delegation to the Worker, so a person who became the card's owner later, and neither built nor delegated it, sees Accept enabled (RG-N5-8); ledger and thread entries name people, not "human".

### 2.16 Configuration

`#/configuration` (`g c`), in the System menu and always visible; each section is addressable (`#/configuration/models`, `/benchmark`, `/review`, `/preferences`, `/project`; the older `/browser` still opens Preferences), `#/registry` opens Benchmark and `#/settings` opens Preferences. It is reached from navigation, the palette (*Configuration*, *Set up models*, *Benchmark models*), the first run when no model is set up (§2.2.5, [surface](surface.md) item 5b), the *No Coding model* bar, each role on Machine, and every message that says a role is not configured (the Reviewer's unfilled-role reason in Review links here). It replaces the Registry and Settings views (owner decisions O2 and O3, [DEC-29](../DECISIONS.md#dec-29--the-owners-answers-to-the-decision-queue)); the scan, the recommendations and the download are [models](models.md)'s (NEW-models-12), the benchmark's mechanics [measurement](measurement.md)'s (NEW-measurement-5), and the shapes [PM_CONTRACT §3](../PM_CONTRACT.md#3-endpoints) *Configuration*. A lede, once: *Choose which models do each job, and compare them on this machine. Nothing is downloaded, loaded or run until you press the button.*

**Layout** (NEW-dashboard-19; DEC-51 adopts the approved Configuration mockup's cards; FINDINGS CFG-07). Under the title, one **machine line** in plain words (*This Mac · Apple M4 · 24 GB unified memory · about 120 GB/s memory bandwidth*, from [models](models.md)' probe). The sections are one underlined tab bar — Models, Benchmark, Review capacity, Preferences, Project — the same tab treatment as the issue page's (§2.6), never small boxed buttons. Inside a section each part is one card on the page, with no box inside a card: Models holds **Model library** (the model folders of item 1, with *Add folder*, *Scan*, the last scan's line and the read-only note), **Available models** (the *Models found* table: model and family, parameters, quantisation, size, speed with its grade — *Measured* or *Estimated*, item 1a —, fits, and where the specs came from, [models](models.md) MD-N13-2), **Suggested setup** (each role, its job, the recommended model, *Keep* or *Change*, and the reason in one sentence; the checks it meets in words — *Fits in memory*, *Verified on this machine*, *Review model from another family*; **Apply suggestion** (primary), which is item 1's *Use the recommended models* with its one confirmation, and *Choose each role*) and **Compare setups** (the benchmark's *Run quick* with its estimate and *Schedule overnight*, item 2). The suggestion is the recommendation rule's ([models](models.md) NEW-models-12), so the card is not titled as Seshat's, as the mockup titles it: no model chose it.

1. **Models.**
   - **Model folders**: each configured folder with where it was set (configuration file, `--models-dir`, `SEKHEMET_MODELS_DIR`), whether it could be read (and why not), and how many models it holds. **Add folder** offers the likely folders that exist on this machine as one-click choices, and a path field; **Remove**; **Scan** re-reads every folder, with progress per folder. A folder is only read, never written.
   - **Models found**: a table of every model the scan found — name, size in GB, quantisation, context length in tokens, family, **Fits this machine** (*Yes*, *Yes, swaps with the other roles*, or *Needs N GB*, with the reason in words: *13 GB of 16 GB usable*), and whether it matches the registry's published hash (*Verified*, *Not a registry model*, *Hash differs*). A model that does not fit is listed, never hidden.
   - **Roles**, always in the order Coding model, Planning model, Review model, Research model (DEC-31; internally Worker, Planner, Reviewer, Researcher), the Planning model row saying *Seshat, the project manager, runs on this model*: each role's **current model by name**, its state (*Resident*, *Swapped out*, *Not configured*), its benchmark score when there is one, and the **recommended model with its reason** in one plain sentence (*Recommended: Gemma-4-26B-A4B. It is a different family from the Coding model, which the Review model needs, and it fits at 14 GB.*). Actions: **Assign** a found model to the role, **Load**, **Unload**, and, for a recommended model that is not present, **Download…**. A role left unfilled says why in words (the Reviewer's reason is [review-git §2.3.7](review-git.md)'s).
   - **Assign** follows the models spec's adoption rule ([models](models.md) rule 30a): a person may assign any model verified on this machine (*qualified*, internally), with or without a benchmark; where the model is not yet verified on this machine, the button reads **Verify on this machine to assign** (a check of a few minutes: tool calls, a multi-turn tool conversation, recall — [models](models.md) rule 27a) and assigns when it passes. The Review model refuses a model of the Coding model's family, saying so. The previous assignment is kept and **Restore previous** brings it back.
   - **Load** and **Unload** go through the residency scheduler, never around the memory guard: a load that would not fit says why and which model would be unloaded; unloading a model a running card is using waits for the next step boundary, and the row says so (*The agent stops after step 5, then unloads*).
   - **Download…** never starts on its own. It opens a confirmation with the model, its source host, its size, the published SHA-256 (mono, with Copy), the folder it will be written to with its free space (the first configured folder that is there and writable now — an unplugged drive's folder is never offered, never created, and the server refuses any folder but a configured, present, writable one), and one line on what leaves the machine (*A request for this file to its source host. Nothing about your project is sent.*); then **Download** (primary) or Cancel. Progress shows bytes, rate and time left, then *Verifying…*, then *Verified. Ready to assign.* — or *The file's hash didn't match the published one, so it was deleted.* When `[network] mode` does not allow the source host, Download is disabled with the setting named; a model with no registered source and hash offers no download. A file of the same name already in the folder is never replaced: the confirmation asks for another name. It is the same implementation as `sekhemet models fetch <model>` ([models](models.md) NEW-models-7).
   - **Use the recommended models** does all of this in one path for a person who does not want to choose: one confirmation listing every download it needs (each with source, size and hash) and the folder they go to — the confirmation names back each download's model and hash and the folder, and the server refuses it when they differ from the plan it recomputes — then the downloads, then the quick benchmark of the recommended combination, then the assignment of each recommended model that is verified on this machine (a model that is not yet verified gets its check first, [models](models.md) rule 27a), and a sentence saying what was done and anything it could not do.
   - **Three layers** (NEW-dashboard-27; W18 G1; C3-3 under DEC-56). **(1) The simple setup card** — *Suggested setup*, above: one row per role with its job, its current model, the recommended model, **Keep** or **Change**, the checks in words (*Fits in memory*, *Verified on this machine*, *Needs verifying*, *Review model from another family*) and **Customize**; under the rows **Apply suggestion** (*Use the recommended models*), whose one confirmation states the recommended set's total size and each model's licence before the yes (models MD-N18-3, MD-N22-3). **(2) Customize**, per role: five tabs — **Basics** (the model, a context slider whose fit is recomputed as it moves, with nothing loaded: *13.2 GB of 16.0 GB usable*, and *Fast*, *Balanced*, *Careful*), **Sampling** (temperature, top-p, top-k, min-p, repeat and presence penalty, seed), **Reasoning** (thinking policy, level, cap, and the floor, read-only), **Engine** (KV type, flash attention, GPU layers, slots, MTP, load mode) and **Harness** (tool arm, working method, evidence check — the evidence gate, internally — step budget). **(3) Every value graded** in words beside it — *Measured*, *From its makers* (the model card's values; DEC-31 keeps *card* for a board's tile, so the page says *makers*), *Estimated*, *Default*, or *Set by <person>* with the date — with **Reset** beside each value a person set. **Export** and **Import** move one role's settings as a file. At the top of the *Model library* card, the **Inference engine** as one part of it (models rule 6b, NEW-models-19), so Models keeps the mockup's four cards (DB-N19-5); in the Team setup each role names its engine service's check in words (*answering on port 8098, matches its profile*; MD-N15-3). **Compare setups** runs the quick benchmark of the suggested combination from the card, with its estimate, and links to Benchmark for the rest.
1a. **Model details, combinations and placement** — Smart Swap's part of Models ([DEC-45](../DECISIONS.md#dec-45--smart-swap); [models](models.md) rules 20d–20k; built in B4.1). It helps a person choose models, engines, placement and combinations from measured facts, as part of this section, not a new view. **Every number carries its grade**, shown beside it in words: *Measured* (recorded on this machine by a run), *From the file* (read from the model's file itself — its size, its header — never called *Measured*), *Estimated* (computed before a measurement, with its spread where it has one) or *Design value* (a D value of ours, not yet tuned by replay).
   - **Per model** (opening a row of Models found):
     - **identity:** family, total and active parameters, quantisation, engine (GGUF on llama.cpp; MLX), size, maximum context, licence, source and hash;
     - **memory:** weights, KV at the role's context and KV type, compute buffer and prompt cache, set against the live headroom (the lower of the GPU and system measures, [models](models.md) rule 20g), with what-ifs for the context size and the KV type that recompute it without loading anything;
     - **speed:** decode and prefill per engine — first estimated (efficiency × bandwidth ÷ bytes per token, [models](models.md) rule 20d), then measured with llama-bench (one warm-up, five runs at the role's depth, accepted at a spread of 3% or less) when a person presses **Measure speed…** and confirms the model and the memory it loads; time to first token with and without the prefix cache, from the same action; cold and warm load time per volume, with the placement advice;
     - **per role:** qualification status for the full combination (engine build, template, MTP and the rest, [models](models.md) rule 27a), quick-benchmark scores, tool-call validity, and the record on the person's own projects;
     - **warnings,** in words: won't fit; slow drive; unqualified; licence; Ollama requantisation; an aging cap that cannot be met (the predicted wait shown instead);
     - **before a download:** memory and speed estimated from a remote GGUF's metadata alone — by our own estimator over `@huggingface/gguf`, each range answered with a 206 and the bytes read capped — with no weights fetched. gguf-parser-go is given a local file only (`--path`), never its `--url`, which would fetch outside the `[network]` policy.
   - **Per combination** (an assignment of models to roles): the hard filters (the Reviewer's family differs from the Worker's; everything fits), each exclusion with its reason; peak memory and what co-resides; C_pair for each role pair; predicted wait p50 and p90 per class, Seshat's included; **time per card** = E[attempts] × compute + E[swaps] × C_pair, E[swaps] from a closed-loop replay of the person's last N cards under this combination (median and p90 with N; *Estimated* while N < 20); and accepted cards per night. Combinations are ordered by every role's quality floor, then the least time per card, then the smaller footprint; **no weighted combined score** is computed or shown ([models](models.md) rule 4c).
   - **Placement:** a what-if per model on a slower volume — *Copy to internal storage: saves about 6 swaps' worth of loading, 40 minutes a day* — chosen as a knapsack by swaps per day × load time saved ÷ GB, keeping at least 20 GB of internal space free; a copy that would break that is not suggested, and the page says why. **Copy to internal storage…** opens a confirmation with the model, its size, the destination, the free space left after it, and the published or recorded SHA-256; nothing is copied until the person presses **Copy**. The copy is verified by hash before the registry points to it; a copy whose hash differs is deleted and the registry is unchanged; the original is never deleted.
   - **Across the page:** a 24-hour residency timeline from the recorded loads and unloads, with the realised θ per hour; an engine comparison per model (its llama.cpp and MLX builds, where both are found); and every benchmark run, quick and full (finalists on the role's qualification cases), stored so the page improves with use.
2. **Benchmark — two tiers for comparing model combinations.** People mix and match one model per role from the models they have, up to the strongest this machine can run, to find the best combination. The **quick** tier answers in minutes; the **overnight** tier settles what the quick tier could not tell apart. The page says so once, in these words: *The quick benchmark shows speed, fit and large differences; the overnight benchmark settles close calls.* The mechanics of both are [measurement](measurement.md)'s (NEW-measurement-5); applying a winning combination is always a person's choice.
   - **Combination builder**: one picker per role (Coding, Planning, Review and Research model), each offering only the models that fit this machine — a model that does not fit is shown disabled with *Needs N GB* — and showing, for each model, its cached quick score for that role with its interval, or *No quick score yet*; a role whose screen is not built yet shows *Not measured yet* instead, for every model (the Reviewer's screen arrives with B4.8 and the Researcher's with B4.4, [measurement](measurement.md) rule 30a). The combination in use is pre-filled and marked *Current*; the recommended one is one click away.
   - **Run quick** carries its estimate in minutes, stated before anything runs and computed from what is not yet cached: at most about 17 minutes for a new Coding model, its load included (6 cards, each capped at 2 minutes); at most about 5 minutes for each new Planning, Review or Research model; at most about 10 minutes for the end-to-end check; so under about 45 minutes for a whole combination with nothing cached ([measurement](measurement.md) rule 32); an estimate over a target is marked before the person confirms, and *Already measured: no run needed* shows when every role is cached. Each role is scored once per candidate model and cached, so changing one role re-runs only that role; a combination's score is assembled from its roles plus a short end-to-end check.
   - **Schedule overnight** runs a thorough, paired, repeated comparison of the combinations the person picks — by default the top combinations the quick tier could not separate, up to 3 — and says how many fit tonight's window, from about 5 hours per combination (recorded throughput when there is one): *Tonight 22:00–03:00 (5 h): 1 of 2 combinations fits; the other runs tomorrow night*. It runs only in the machine's overnight window ([models](models.md) rule 20b), stops at the window's end and resumes the next night, and never runs while a card runs or while the machine is reserved — inside reserved hours even when the person is idle, or after *Reserve now* (§2.11).
   - **State and Stop**: each run is *Queued* (with when it will start), *Running* (the tier, which role and model or which combination is being measured, cards done of total, elapsed and time left), *Stopped* or *Done*, and has **Stop**, which ends it and keeps every result already measured, marked partial. One run at a time: while another run holds the runner, Run quick is disabled and names it, and an overnight run waits.
   - **Comparison**: a table of the combinations measured on this machine, one row each — the model per role, each role's score, the end-to-end check's result, the combination's score with its interval, and its tier — sorted by score, with the recommended combination marked and its reason. **Two candidates are labelled *No clear difference* (DEC-31; *indistinguishable*, internally) when the paired sign test on the same items does not reject** — two models for a role on the cards, briefs, defects or questions both ran, and two combinations on each role in which their models differ ([measurement](measurement.md) rules 10 and 35); interval overlap decides nothing. Candidates with no clear difference are shown as tied, and the page never ranks one above another it cannot tell apart; every score keeps its interval. Beside each role's score are its **secondary measures** — seconds per card, valid tool calls, steps — which is where the quick tier tells close models apart. A role that reads *Not measured yet* is left out of the comparison, and the table says so. After an overnight run the table leads with the **morning report**: which differences the overnight comparison resolved (*A is better than B*) and which still show *No clear difference*, in words. A row whose models are all verified on this machine ([models](models.md) rule 27a) offers **Assign this combination**; a row with a model not yet verified offers **Verify on this machine to assign** instead. A benchmark is never required to assign (rule 30a), and nothing is assigned without that press.
   - **History**, per combination: every recorded run of either tier with its date, settings and scores, and the recorded frozen baseline for reference, compared with [measurement](measurement.md)'s paired statistics (*Better than the baseline*, *Worse*, or *Not established: the difference is smaller than the suite can show*, never a bare difference). It absorbs the bake-off matrix by hardware tier that Registry showed (`writeBakeOffMatrix`).
   - **Settings in the combination** (W18 G4; [measurement](measurement.md) rule 39, NEW-measurement-8). Beside each role's model picker, a **settings** picker: *As set* (the values the role runs at now), or the settings a *Find best settings* run found for that model, each named in words (*temperature 0.2*, *reasoning medium, cap 8,192*). Only values a run can apply without its own load are offered; context, KV type and the engine's values are set on Models › Customize. A combination with settings is its own row in the comparison, its settings in words beside its models.
   - **Find best settings** (W18 G3; [measurement](measurement.md) rule 38, NEW-measurement-7), one card per role whose screen is built (Coding and Review; the others say why they cannot). It shows the model, the candidates in words, the estimate in minutes and what it checks first (*the host's memory, then the runner*). **Find best settings** opens a confirmation; nothing runs before it. While running it shows the rung and the candidates left, with **Stop**. Then it shows the verdict in words: *Best combination: temperature 0.2 — higher on 6 of 6 issues (p = 0.031)*; *Faster, not established: …*; or *No clear difference: the screen could not tell the settings apart.* **Apply** is a person's press; it is disabled, with the reason, when there is nothing to apply. After no clear difference, **Settle overnight** schedules the two settings as two combinations.
   - **History and charts** (rule 39). Per combination, every recorded run of both tiers with the date, settings and scores, each against the run before it (*Better*, *Worse*, *No clear difference*). Two inline SVG charts — the score of each run with its item range, and the role scores side by side — each with a table holding the same numbers. No chart library.
   - **Other benchmarks.** The capstone and Web-Bench results from their recorded `score.json` files, each with its arm, run, scores and validity, and its protocol in words: for the capstone, the frozen byte-identical input, the hidden suite and two to three runs per arm; for Web-Bench, the project and commit, tasks in order, one retry with the test output, pass@1 and pass@2. With none recorded each says so: *No capstone result is recorded on this machine yet.*, *No Web-Bench result is recorded on this machine yet.*
   - **Every control explains itself** (FINDINGS CFG-10, HC-24). A disabled control says why beside it. *Run quick* names what is missing (*Choose a Coding model and a Planning model*), the run holding the runner, or the model that does not fit. *Schedule overnight* is disabled with its reason before the press — no combination to compare, a model that does not fit — instead of failing after it. A model that does not fit is disabled in every picker with *Needs N GB*. A person without `config.manage` sees every control disabled, with the level note once at the top (DB-N6-15).
3. **Review capacity**: review minutes per day, editable by a person holding the Accept permission, or by an Admin in the Team setup; the change is a recorded config event and ReviewWIP is recomputed at once and shown with its derivation (*limit 4, from 60 review minutes a day at ~15 min per card*). A value of 0 or less is refused beside the field ([review-git §2.2.3](review-git.md)).
4. **Preferences** (DEC-31; formerly *This browser*): theme, density, Tips and first-run role, kept per browser; and **Estimation** — *Off* (the default) or *Story points* — kept per project (`project/settings_changed`, `PATCH /api/projects/:id/settings { estimation }`), changed by the project's lead or an Admin (`project.settings`), read-only with that reason for anyone else (DB-N7-2); and **Apply Seshat's suggestions automatically** — Labels, Priority, Duplicate link, Split, each off until an Admin turns it on for the project (`PATCH /api/projects/:id/settings { auto_apply }`, `auto_apply.enable`), read-only with that reason below Admin, never the assignee or health (`autoApplyView`, `packages/ui/src/settings.ts`; [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) PM-N9-2, [teams](teams.md) TEAM-41). A suggestion a rule applied shows on the issue as *Applied by <Admin>'s rule: …* with *Undo*, and in its Activity (B4.8).

   **Browser notifications** (NEW-dashboard-22; DEC-53 c4; FINDINGS PRC-11), kept per browser like theme: *Notify me in this browser when work waits for me*. The browser's permission prompt appears only after the person presses **Turn on**, never on load. The dashboard also offers it once, as one line above the Review queue the first time the queue is non-empty in that browser (*Get a browser notification when work waits for you.* **Turn on** · *Not now*); *Not now* is remembered and the line is not shown again. While it is on and a dashboard tab is open but not in front, an issue entering In review — in the Team setup, an item reaching the person's Inbox under *Needs you* or *Review requested* — raises one notification: the issue's key and title and where it waits (*CHR-12 Implement canonical JSON · waiting in In review*), never code, a diff or a model's text. Several within 60 seconds raise one (*3 issues wait in In review*). Clicking it brings the tab to the front and opens the issue in Review. When the browser has blocked notifications, the setting says so and how to allow them in the browser's site settings. **On this computer when the dashboard is closed** (Solo only; DEC-53 c4's smallest proposal, the D1 review): a second switch, *Also notify me on this computer when no dashboard tab is open*, off until the person turns it on, kept in the user configuration (`[notify] desktop`, default `false`) because the server, not the browser, raises it. While it is on and no dashboard tab holds the live stream, the same events raise one operating-system notification through the system's own tool — `osascript` (`display notification`) on macOS, `notify-send` on Linux where present — with the same words, the same 60-second batching and no code, diff or model text. The issue's title is passed to the tool as an argument, never spliced into a script or a shell command. Where neither tool exists the switch is disabled and says why. In the Team setup the server is not the person's computer, so the switch is not offered; a notification integration covers that case ([integrations](integrations.md) items 21–23a). No library.
5. **Project configuration**: first, the **Definition of done** (NEW-dashboard-14; FINDINGS PRC-12), read-only and in plain words, built from `gates.toml` (the checks an issue must pass), the depth profile (the test-strength rule and who approves tests, [design-stage](design-stage.md) §2.8) and the Accept rule (who may accept, [teams](teams.md) item 7) — *An issue is done when its checks pass — Types, Tests, Lint, Size — its tests meet the strength rule, and a person the Accept rule names accepts it.* It states what is enforced and enforces nothing new. Then the effective configuration, read-only, each value with where it came from (default, user file, project file, flag, environment) and where to change it; above it, **Agent queue** — *Agent issues per person*, the queue's per-person cap ([teams](teams.md) item 30), with its source, which an Admin saves to the user configuration (`PUT /api/config/queue`, `queue.caps`; recorded as the Admin's configuration change) and anyone else sees read-only with the level note beside it.
   **Network activity** (NEW-dashboard-24; [security](security.md) item 33a, NEW-security-11; FINDINGS INS-08), the last card of Project configuration (`#/configuration/project`): every recorded `harness/egress`, `card/egress` and `model/downloaded` event of the project, newest first — the time, the host, its purpose, *Allowed* or *Refused*, the size, and the issue or person that caused it (an issue as its key and title, linked). Filters: *Since* (a date), *Refused only*. With none recorded the card says *Nothing has left this machine.* Below the list, one line links the user guide's *Privacy and network* page, which lists every host the product can reach. The card reads existing events and records nothing; it shows a person only what their access level lets them read, and an erased private part as erased. It is read-only for everyone, with no action. It lives in the Project section, not a sixth tab, because the approved mockup fixes the tab bar (Layout above; DB-N19-5) and the events are the project's ledger's (the D1 review resolved security item 33a's *Configuration › Privacy*, which no tab holds, this way).

**Who may change it.** In the Team setup every change to the server's configuration on this page — Models, Benchmark, Project configuration, the queue's per-person Agent cap and slots ([teams](teams.md) items 30–32) — needs the **Admin** level, and Review capacity needs an Admin or a person the project's Accept rule names (superseded by DEC-35: before it, every section needed the Accept permission); each change is recorded with the person who made it; a person without the level sees those sections read-only, with that reason beside each control, and folders are paths on the server. **Preferences** is the exception for its browser settings: theme, density, Tips and first-run role are that browser's own, so anyone may change them, with no permission and nothing recorded on the server; its Estimation is the project's and needs `project.settings`. The page names each role's model in plain words; mono ids appear only in the Models found table and the download confirmation.

### 2.17 Sign in, Inbox, My issues, Members and Audit

The pages the Team setup adds, and the Inbox both setups share ([DEC-35](../DECISIONS.md#dec-35--one-product-two-setups-solo-and-team)). Behaviour is [teams](teams.md)'s; this section owns how they look. Each keeps §2.13's standard: one list or table, terse labels, one primary action.

1. **Sign in** (`#/signin`; Team only — Solo has no sign-in, [teams](teams.md) item 1). A centred 360 px column on `--bg-base`: the brand lockup; **Continue with company SSO** and **Sign in with a passkey**, each shown only when an Admin has turned it on (DEC-38); email and password, with **Sign in** as the primary action; *Ask an admin for an invite link* in place of a sign-up form (a sign-up form appears only for a domain an Admin opened, and its accounts read *Pending until an Admin approves it*, item 10); and the footer line *Self-hosted · your code and data stay on this server*. On the first start it is **Set up Sekhemet**, asking for the setup token from the file whose path the server console printed (item 2). An invite link opens a page that shows the invite (workspace, level, who sent it) with **Accept invite** as the only action; opening the link consumes nothing (item 10).
2. **Inbox** (`#/inbox`, `g x`; both setups) — what reached the person, across projects, grouped by reason: *Needs you*, *Mentioned*, *Review requested*, *Watching*, *Agent finished* ([teams](teams.md) item 24). Each row: the issue key and title, the project, who or what caused it (an AI teammate with its badge and its state — *queued*, *working*, *needs you*, *paused*, *done*, *failed*, item 19), and the time; actions *Done*, *Snooze* (until a time) and *Save*, and tabs *All*, *Saved* and *Done*. Empty: *Nothing needs you.* **Two panes in a window 1100 px and wider** (the window's width, as §2.2.2; NEW-dashboard-19; DEC-51, as Linear's inbox does, adopting the approved Inbox mockup; FINDINGS TEAM-03 — before it, one grouped list whose rows left the Inbox): the grouped list on the left and, on the right, a **reading pane** for the selected item — the crumb (project / key), *Snooze*, *Save* and *Done*, the title, a line with the issue's column, assignee, delegate and release (board column words, DEC-52), what caused the item in context (the comment or question, the checks' result, or the plan sent for approval), and a reply box (*Type @ to mention a person or the Agent*) with **Reply**, and **Open issue**. Selecting a row (click or `j/k`, which move focus as well as the selection; FINDINGS TEAM-01) fills the pane and leaves the person in the Inbox; a *Needs you* item's pane holds its decision with its options and *Answer*, or *Review it* for work in Review, and *Open issue* opens Review › *Needs you* or the issue page. **Narrower than 1100 px it is one list** — on a phone and from 768 to 1099 px alike —, and an item opens its reading pane in place of the list, with *Back to Inbox*, which returns to the same row.
3. **My issues** (`#/my-issues`, `g y`; Team only) — the issues the person owns, is delegated or is asked to review, across the projects they can see, in the List view's table (§2.4.15) grouped by project.
4. **Members** (`#/members`; from the account menu) — a table: the person (initials avatar, name, email, presence dot), access level, per-project overrides, profile label and last active. An Admin sees **Invite** (primary), which creates a single-use link with a level, an optional project and an expiry (7 days by default) and shows it once to copy; per row, change level, override for a project, change label, issue a password reset, unlock a locked account and remove; and pending people — from open sign-up or first seen through the identity proxy — with *Approve* and *Decline* ([teams](teams.md) items 6–10). Others see the table read-only. **Parts** (NEW-dashboard-19; DEC-51 adopts the approved Members mockup; FINDINGS TEAM-04): tabs **Members**, **Invites** and **Sign-in**, and an *Audit log* link for an Admin. The Members table's columns are Name, Access, Labels, Projects and **Can accept in** (the projects whose Accept rule names the person, [teams](teams.md) item 7). Under it, **AI teammates** — Seshat and the Agent with their *AI* badges, *not members · no access level · no seat*, each with its role, what it may do (Seshat: *Suggests; people apply*, with the auto-apply state, §2.16.4; the Agent: *Acts with the access of the person who starts it*, with the per-person cap, [teams](teams.md) item 30) — and **Access levels**: each level and what it allows, from the same table the server checks ([teams](teams.md) item 6), with the line *Accepting work is set per project. Labels such as Developer or Product owner set a person's home page, not what they can do.* **Invites** (an Admin) lists each outstanding invite — its email, level, project, who sent it and when it expires — with **Revoke** (`GET` and `DELETE /api/invites`, [teams](teams.md) item 10); an invite is a credential, so nobody else sees the tab. **Sign-in** shows the sign-in settings in force — company SSO, passkeys, passwords and their minimum length, open sign-up, session lengths — each with where it is changed ([teams](teams.md) items 11–14).
5. **Audit log** (`#/audit`; Admin only; titled as the Members mockup and GitHub title it, §8 question 8) — the activity log read as a table: time, actor (an AI teammate with its badge and *on behalf of* the person who started it), action and target; filters by person, action and project; **Export** as CSV or JSON. It includes every sign-in, level change, invite, model change and configuration change, and is a read of the ledger, not a second store ([teams](teams.md) item 27).

## 3. Contract

| Item | Where |
| --- | --- |
| Labels, `statusLine`, `describeCard`, `columnLabel`, `stopReasonLabel`, `BOARD_COLUMN_ORDER`, `COLUMN_EMPTY`; `BOARD_COLUMNS` (the five professional columns and On hold, each `{ id, label, states, empty, queue, onlyWithCards }`), `PIPELINE_COLUMNS` (the nine stored states), `WONT_DO_COLUMN`, `boardColumnOf(status)` (`backlog` · `todo` · `in_progress` · `in_review` · `done` · `on_hold`, and `wont_do` for Rejected); `CardDisplay` adds `quiet` (the status only restates the column and budget), `startedAt`, `ownerName`, `delegateName`, and `type` (the issue type's id, DEC-31); new `plainStatus`; the issue types `IssueType` (`story` · `task` · `bug` · `spike` · `epic`), `ISSUE_TYPE_LABELS` (label and tooltip), `issueTypeOf(card)`, `issueTypeLabel` — the retired `KIND_LABELS` and `kindLabel` are gone; `vocabularyTables` publishes `types` in place of `kinds` | `packages/ui/src/vocabulary.ts` |
| The board model: `boardModel({ cards, visible, now, pipeline, wipLimits, reviewLimit, expanded, collapsed, sort, wontDo, estimation })` → `{ columns, chips }` (each column's cards in order, count, points and `pointsText` — empty unless `estimation` is `points`, DB-N7-2 —, `limit { count, limit, state, text, derivation }`, `countTone`), `boardColumnDefs({ pipeline, wontDo })` (the columns in force for the board and its swimlanes alike; *Won't do* before On hold), `onColumns(cards, defs)` (the cards some column shows: what a lane holds and counts), `reviewLimitText`, `sortColumn`, `focusAfterFrame`, `readPipeline` / `writePipeline` (`PIPELINE_KEY`), `readColumnChoices(storage, pipeline)` / `writeColumnChoices` (`COLUMN_CHOICES_KEY`: each column's sort and the folded and opened columns, per browser and per layout) | `packages/ui/src/columns.ts` (`/app/lib/columns.js`) |
| The tile model: `tileModel(card, { now, epics, pmPaused, estimation })` → key, type (the issue type's icon and word, DEC-31), points (only with `estimation: "points"`), owner, delegate (*Agent* or a person), title, priority, epic, labels, status (no step count, DB-N7-3), blocker (the planner's criteria-approval hold in the board's words: *open the card to approve them*, never the CLI command), age, pips — no step budget | `packages/ui/src/tiles.ts` (`/app/lib/tiles.js`) |
| The story map model: `storyMapModel({ map, cards, epics })` → `{ backbone, bands: { id, heading, skeleton, stateText, cells: { epicId, requirements (each with `moscow`: Must have, Should have or Could have, by Status's `moscowOf`), cards } }, note?, empty?, provenLine? }`; `REQUIREMENT_STATES`, `requirementState` | `packages/ui/src/storymap.ts` (`/app/lib/storymap.js`) |
| The burn-up: `burnupChart(series, width, extras?)` → paths, ticks, end labels, caption and table rows in the series' `unit` (points, or issues), or `{ empty }`; `extras { band?: { from, to }, target? }` (Status's forecast range and target date, §2.8.3) adds `band { x1, x2, label }` and `target { x, label }`, widens the span to reach them and says them in the caption, drawn by `web/burnup.js` `burnupHtml(state, width, extras)`; `cycleHeaderShown(cycles, filter, now)`; `burnupTarget(cycles, filter, now, projectId?, estimation?)` → the sprint in force or the project (the one the board is scoped to, `&project=`), with its URL, `&unit=issues` unless estimation is story points | `packages/ui/src/burnup.ts` (`/app/lib/burnup.js`) |
| Status (§2.8, DB-P5-1, DB-P5-2, DB-N9-1..8): `statusModel({ now, me?, facts, cards, cycles?, storyMap?, standup?, signals?, standing? })` → `{ headline, health, setHealth, healthChoices, target, setTarget, update, updateMissing, writeUpdate, numbers: { id, label, value, detail?, jump? }[], appetite?, forecast: { band?, target? }, needsYou: { items: { kind, text, buttons: { label, href? \| act?, id?, ids?, cards? }[] }[], empty }, waiting, requirements: { groups: { label, summary, rows }[], empty? }, risks: { items: { sentence, suggested, why, action? }[], empty }, standup, doneThisWeek, working: { rows: { who, ai, item }[] }, models, flow: { items: { label, value, href }[], note? } }`; `StatusFacts` (the shape of `GET /api/status`, with `canUnpark`), `requirementWord`, `moscowOf` (the project documents' rule), `HEALTH_LABELS`, `STATUS_SECTIONS` (§2.8's order), `STATUS_COPY` (every other word) | `packages/ui/src/status.ts` (`/app/lib/status.js`); the server half `statusFacts` in `apps/harness/src/status_api.ts` |
| Quick create: `QUICK_CREATE_COPY`, `quickCreateRequest({ title, description?, epicId?, projectId? })` (the server validates with the same function; `projectId` the project the board is scoped to), `epicFromFilter` | `packages/ui/src/create.ts` (`/app/lib/create.js`) |
| The reach check (DB-P3-17): `BOARD_GEOMETRY`, `boardLayout(columns, viewport)`, `blockedCardPaths(model, viewport, { now })` | `packages/ui/src/reach.ts` (`/app/lib/reach.js`) |
| The live step (NEW-dashboard-3): `tokensFrame(raw)`, `onTokensFrame(prev, frame, { cardId, tab, running })` (null outside the Steps tab of a running card), `onStepEvent(prev, event)`, `liveRow(tokens, { step, stepBudget })`, `LIVE_STEP_COPY`, `LIVE_TEXT_LIMIT` (2,000) | `packages/ui/src/live.ts` (`/app/lib/live.js`) |

| Evidence that stays readable (NEW-dashboard-1): `gateStripModel(gates, layers)` → `{ grouped, segments: { key, family?, label, state, gates, count, failing, overflow }[] }` (one segment per gate up to `STRIP_GROUP_AFTER` 6; past it one per family, failing first), `CHECK_FAMILY_LABELS`, `stateBadge(status, tone)` → `{ text, tone, mark? }` (the issue page's pill: the board column's name, or *Needs a new plan* / *Checks failed* / *Will retry*, never a failure mark on a stage); `gateNameOf(id, rung?)` and `boardColumnLabel(status)` in `vocabulary.ts` | `packages/ui/src/strip.ts` (`/app/lib/strip.js`) |

| Icons, the brand mark and the AI badge (DB-N9-18, -19): `ICONS` (the 1.5 px line set; the old `glyph` is gone), `brandMark(size)` (pylons `fill:var(--text-primary)`, disc `fill:var(--accent)`, never under `BRAND_MARK_MIN_PX` 16), `brandLockup(size)` (mark and wordmark, `min-width` `BRAND_LOCKUP_MIN_PX` 80), `APP_ICON` and `appIconSvg()` (the favicon: ink tile `#1D1B17`, cream pylons `#F3EEE3`, gold disc `#D9B45A`), `AI_BADGE`, `aiBadge()`, `teammateName(name, kind)` (the badge after Seshat's or the Agent's name, never a person's) | `packages/ui/src/icons.ts` (`/app/lib/icons.js`, re-exported by `dom.js`) |

any other type is read word by word through the same table (`eventSentence`, `plainEventWords`, `fieldWords` in `vocabulary.ts`). `vocabulary.spec.ts` reads the sentence of every registered event type and of those a real workspace records unregistered (`gate/result`, `card/budget_set`, `cycle/created`, …); `professional_words_ui.spec.ts` seeds a sprint, a release, a delegate, a check, a step budget and a field edit and reads `#/ledger` at 1440 and 400 px (the mono Type column, an identifier this spec keeps, is not read as copy). **R-50 (C2a fix):** the two `stripThinking` wrappers (`pm/agent.ts`, `research/apodex.ts`) and `researcher.ts`'s `strip` alias are gone; every caller reads `stripReasoning` from `@sekhemet/models` (MD-N4-8). **R-49, partly:** C2a's own tests are named `<module>_<behaviour>.spec.ts` — `board_edits_undo_api`, `board_layout_tiles_ui`, `issue_page_api`, `issue_review_page_ui`, `professional_words_api`, `professional_words_ui`, `start_page_draft_ui`, `workspace_status_api`, `shell_status_states_ui`, `members_invites_api`, `inbox_members_visual_ui`, and in `packages/ui` `issue_review_states`, `status_switchers`, `inbox_members_brand`; **left:** the milestone-named files that predate C2a (`*_b41`, `*_b43`, `*_b44`, `*_b48`, `*_b411`, `b21_*`, `b22_*`, `seshat_p6`, `cuts_b0`, `wave2*`, `*_wave2*`), because renaming them rewrites the front matter and State rows of most other specs and files other workstreams own — a mechanical pass for its own commit (the plan's cheaper-model work). **R-41, left:** `wave2.ts` (2,176 lines) is still one module; splitting it into `plan_command.ts`, `queue_order.ts`, `dev_commands.ts` and `fixture_gate.ts` moves the CLI's planning and queue code and its importers, none of them a dashboard surface, so it waits for the session that next works inside that file (CLAUDE.md: strangler fig, behind tests).
