## 7. Later

- **Rewind from the card view** ("rewind to step N": reset the worktree to that checkpoint, record the rewind, invalidate later gate passes; it truncates nothing). `sekhemet rewind` and `fork` exist on the CLI ([kernel](kernel.md)); the card view offers them after v1.
- **Retry with planner** from Review (`POST /api/cards/:id/run`) — in v1 Send back returns the card to Ready with the person's note in the next attempt's dossier ([review-git](review-git.md) §2.4), which is the retry; a second retry path from Review would re-run without the note that makes the retry measurable. The `run` route itself is v1 ([runtime](runtime.md) §3).
- **Saved views on the server** (a views endpoint), so a team shares a filter — Later, [DEC-53](../DECISIONS.md#dec-53--design_gaps-c-decided) (c14; FINDINGS BRD-08); **undo for applied proposals**.
- **A timeline or roadmap across releases** — Later, DEC-53 (c14; FINDINGS PRC-18). v1 shows releases on the story map and Status.
- ***Open in editor*** from the issue page or after a take-over — Later, DEC-53 (c14; FINDINGS PRC-20). v1 offers *Copy* on the branch and the worktree path.
- ***Rules used* on the issue page**, from the nearest nested `AGENTS.md` — v1.x by DEC-53 c12: it changes what the model is told, so it ships only after its A/B under [PROMPT_STANDARD](../PROMPT_STANDARD.md) rule 35. In v1 the issue page names no rule set.
- *(Moved into v1 by [DEC-57](../DECISIONS.md#dec-57--a-server-is-a-workspace-with-many-projects): more than one project per server, NEW-dashboard-25 and -26. D1 had it in v1.x by DEC-53 c3.)*
- **Dependency lines over the board**; **pan and zoom, and the critical path, on the dependency graph** (v1 lays it out in layers); **master board across workspaces** beyond the Projects page; **goal view** with the strategy graph and risk register. Goals are CLI and API in v1 (`sekhemet goal`, `goal status`; [planner-pm](planner-pm.md) §2.11), and the dashboard shows no goal yet — its burn-up is per cycle and per project (§2.4 item 17, §2.8). The view waits until goals are created from the dashboard: v1's non-developer home is Status, which already shows the proven slices, the burn-up and *Needs you*. (The Registry, the other view the old design deferred with these, is v1 as Configuration › Benchmark, §2.16.)
- **A gate-override control in the UI** (with its required reason). v1 records overrides from the CLI and the API only, so the board has no gesture that bypasses a gate.
- **Intent grouping of diffs by difftastic** ("Core interface definition", "Handler implementation", "Acceptance tests") beyond scope- and risk-based grouping.
- **A native wrapper, an IDE extension, a TUI**, and **remote control from a phone beyond read-only status and one-tap triage**. The dashboard is a local web app on loopback, so it already works over an SSH tunnel and on a headless box ([runtime](runtime.md) item 23); a native wrapper adds packaging, not capability. The IDE extension and TUI are in [SPINE](../SPINE.md)'s *Not in v1*.
- **Vendored Inter and JetBrains Mono** (SIL OFL 1.1, as WOFF2), **`@floating-ui/dom`** for popovers and **Lucide** icons — **proposed, needing the owner's yes**; until then the popovers use what is in the repository, and the system font fallbacks must look right. (**Playwright** and **axe-core** are no longer proposals: the owner approved them as development dependencies, O5, and v1 uses them — §8 Q4.)
- **A client-state contract** (`IBoardUIState`: active project, selection, pending decisions, telemetry) — the page keeps this state in `store.js`; a typed contract earns its place when a second client exists.
- **A split of the flow charts by `delegate.kind`** (Worker-built versus person-built) as a view; v1 keeps one WIP and one cycle time per column.

## 8. Open questions

1. **Planning in To do or In progress?** *Recommendation:* To do — nothing is being built yet; the badge says *Being planned*.
2. **Column names in NAMING.** *Resolved:* NAMING now keeps the nine stored names for pipeline view and the stored state, and the five board columns (with On hold and Won't do) for the board; *Working*, *Checking* and *Closed* are retired.
3. **Should the PM's model id be visible?** *Decided* (owner decision O3, [DEC-29](../DECISIONS.md#dec-29--the-owners-answers-to-the-decision-queue)): every role's model name, Seshat's included, is on the Configuration page (§2.16), and the chat panel names no model. This replaces DEC-25 R15, which had kept it in the panel's details and the idle cost line.
4. **Playwright and axe.** *Decided* (owner decision O5): Playwright (Apache-2.0) and axe-core (MPL-2.0, unmodified) are approved as development dependencies; they drive the audience tests (DEFINITION_OF_DONE §6.4), the verification of §2.15.5 and the accessibility check of DB-P12-6. Installed in B4.2 as `playwright-core` 1.61.1 (no browser download; it uses the cached Chromium) and `axe-core` 4.13.0.
5. **Accept friction.** *Decided* (owner decision O13): light — one key per unmet or unclear finding and each Implementation file shown once (§2.5.9; [review-git §2.4.3](review-git.md)).
6. **Self-accept.** *Decided* (owner decision O11): on a project where two or more people hold the Accept permission, neither the builder nor the principal who recorded the latest delegation of the card to the Worker may accept it, whoever owns it now; a project with one Accept-holder is solo ([review-git §2.4.1](review-git.md)). The page shows whatever that rule refuses, with who may accept.
7. **Accept friction in the criteria view** (NEW-dashboard-17). Accept requires each unmet or unclear finding acknowledged and each Implementation file shown once (question 5, O13). A person who manages the work and does not read code still has to open each file before Accept. *Recommendation:* keep the rule unchanged in v1, as DB-N17-2 says — loosening it is not this spec's to do — and write beside Accept *2 files not yet shown* with a link to Changes; whether a person who does not read code should accept code at all, or ask a reviewer, is the owner's question for [review-git](review-git.md), and the measured review rate (§2.10.5) will show how such reviews go.
8. **The Admin's page under DEC-52.** DEC-52 renames the ledger, the event log and the audit trail to *Activity log* on screen, and the Ledger view becomes *Activity log* (§2.11). Naming the Admin's workspace page the same would give two pages one name. *Recommendation:* keep that page *Audit log* (§2.17.5), the industry's name for it (GitHub, Linear) and the approved Members mockup's, and treat DEC-52's *audit trail* as the phrase in running copy; the lead confirms or reverses it.
9. **The triage verb.** Linear's triage action is *Accept*, but in Sekhemet *Accept* is a person's gated acceptance of finished work, recorded as `card/accepted`. *Recommendation:* *Accept into Backlog* (§2.4.19), which records `issue/triaged` and never `card/accepted` (DB-N10-3), so the one word that merges code is not used bare for anything else.

## 9. Evidence and rationale

- Reviews: [domain13_dashboard.md](../../reference/reviews/domain13_dashboard.md) (the professional column mapping, tile anatomy, Learn layer, gap table against Jira/Linear/GitHub) and [domain17_brand_ux.md](../../reference/reviews/domain17_brand_ux.md) (navigation, colour, contrast audit, the three audiences' walk-throughs).
- Practice: the Kanban Guide 2025 requires WIP, throughput, **work item age** and cycle time plus a **service-level expectation** and explicit policies — hence age on tiles, the SLE sentence, and column policies in Learn; NN/g finds pushed tutorials are forgotten while contextual, dismissible, re-findable help works and standard conventions need no explanation — hence the Learn layer's shape; empty states should show status, teach, and offer the next step — [WEB_RESEARCH group C §7](../../research/WEB_RESEARCH_2026-09.md#7-professional-kanban-conventions). Linear's default workflow (Backlog, Todo, In Progress, Done, Canceled), Jira's left-most/right-most column semantics and story points, GitHub's agent statuses on Projects boards, and the human-owner/agent-delegate split — §6 and §7.
- Positioning: Linear Agent already offers PM chat to non-developers, so the dashboard's edge is gates at the column boundary ("accepted means proven"), teaching the practice, and running locally — [group C gap analysis](../../research/WEB_RESEARCH_2026-09.md#gap-analysis).
- Seshat quiet by default, offering in the panel rather than pinging (offering preferred 90% vs 47%; an interruption costs ~23 minutes), capability shown as Wilson intervals from the ledger rather than public leaderboards, and the swap-cost wait explained as steps — [PM_RESEARCH_SYNTHESIS.md](../../research/PM_RESEARCH_SYNTHESIS.md) §1 rows 8, 10, 11.
- Review ergonomics and teams (§2.5.3–9, §2.4.4, §2.4.12): file order changes what is found (Fregnan et al., ESEC/FSE 2022: 64% lower odds for the last file); reviewers follow where an LLM points (arXiv 2411.11401), hence coverage; findings as reminders, not verdicts (Spadini et al., ICSE 2020, search-only); light cognitive forcing (Buçinca et al., CSCW 2021); owner and delegate as two typed fields (Linear) — [DESIGN_RESEARCH_TEAMS_DATA_CHANGE.md](../../research/DESIGN_RESEARCH_TEAMS_DATA_CHANGE.md) §2.1, §2.5. *Passing, strength unmet* and test approval by example table: [DESIGN_RESEARCH_TESTS_BROWNFIELD.md](../../research/DESIGN_RESEARCH_TESTS_BROWNFIELD.md) decisions 1, 5, 6. The team-server research ([DESIGN_RESEARCH_TEAM_SERVER.md](../../research/DESIGN_RESEARCH_TEAM_SERVER.md)) adds nothing page-side beyond the scheduler's fair share, which [runtime](runtime.md) owns.
- What was taken from each tool (priority scale, points, cycles, swimlanes, GitHub query syntax, keyboard triage, Copilot Workspace's editable plan as the proposal, Cursor's per-change apply, Claude's thread typography): the old PM design Part 1 (git `fb59ba2:docs/design/PM_DESIGN.md`).
- Where "done" is drawn (§2.4.17, §2.8.2–5, P13): the requirement graph, reasoning is not evidence, appetite and the circuit breaker, no orphan cards, the story map with a walking skeleton and release slices, and done as proven *and* accepted by a person — [PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH.md](../../research/PROJECT_DONE_AND_DEPTH.md) (via [DEC-11](../DECISIONS.md#dec-11)); the page draws what [planner-pm §2.15](planner-pm.md) computes.
- Working together and Status (§2.2.1, §2.2.6, §2.8, §2.11 Projects, §2.13.8, §2.17, NEW-dashboard-9): [DESIGN_RESEARCH_COLLABORATION.md](../../research/DESIGN_RESEARCH_COLLABORATION.md) §4 (inbox by reason, watch and mention, presence, audit), §5 (sign-in on the team's own hardware) and §6 (a person's health call, the five-part weekly update, the burn-up with a forecast range, flow measures for engineers, no leaderboards); decisions [DEC-35](../DECISIONS.md#dec-35--one-product-two-setups-solo-and-team), [DEC-36](../DECISIONS.md#dec-36--the-ai-is-a-teammate-that-proposes-people-decide), [DEC-37](../DECISIONS.md#dec-37--status-serves-the-stakeholder-and-the-team-from-the-same-data).
- Mockups: `docs/design/mockups/` (board, review, card, runs, pm, board-v2, insights).
- **Resolved drift and deliberate reversals** (each: what changed, why):
  - Server-sent events and the page's own windowing replace the old design's WebSocket and `@tanstack/virtual` — no build step, air-gapped; a React/TanStack stack was rejected for the same reason.
  - Reordering within a column is allowed; moves between columns stay gated.
  - The token values in `tokens.ts` (raised for AA; the parked hue moved to copper) replace the old design's hex table — one source, held by a test.
  - "The tile shows budget, class chip, difficulty and a dependency count" is replaced by the professional anatomy — a tile should read like Jira's; the machine's detail moved to the peek drawer and Facts.
  - Empty columns become chips, not 36 px rails; Done is a full column — rails hid the work people most want to see.
  - A 176 px labelled sidebar replaces the 52 px icon rail at 1024–1279 px — people scan for the words.
  - The phone's tabs are Status · Review · Board · PM, not Review · Board · Runs — the phone is the non-developer's and the reviewer's device.
  - Five primary views are always visible (Status, PM, Review, Board, Insights), not three; Machine sits always in the System menu; Integrations is always listed — the three audiences each need their home, and Integrations is where a connection starts. Insights shows a teaching empty state instead of hiding, because its empty state explains what fills it.
  - No bare `t`; chords reassigned (`g p` PM, `g s` Status, `g i` Insights) — theme is not worth a bare key, and the primary views get the mnemonic letters.
  - The empty Review queue and the empty board offer buttons, not `sekhemet queue` or `sekhemet plan` — no path ends in a terminal.
  - Disabled controls use a neutral fill, not 40% opacity — faded gold read as enabled.
  - The priority glyph moves to row 3 and is hidden for *No priority* (no three dashes) — row 1 is identity (key, points, people), row 3 is planning attributes, and the List view is where priority scans as a column.
  - The story map is a view of the board's cards, not "a separate epic hierarchy screen" — epics still appear as swimlanes, a filter and a column; the map adds the release slices P13 needs.
  - A role never has an avatar (DEC-25 R10): the Worker is a text chip, not a *W* badge; a person keeps their initials. The blocker flag uses the fail tone with an icon and the word *Blocked* (R11); the old dimmed title is dropped, because a dimmed title reads as done or disabled.
  - *Seshat's review* becomes the **Reviewer**'s findings, shown first — the Reviewer is a role, not Seshat (DEC-05), and findings lead because they are reminders of where to look; the "advice, not a gate" line and the severity order are kept.
  - The model id leaves the chat panel entirely (DEC-25 R15, then owner decision O3) — non-developers read it as noise, and the place to see and change which model does each job is Configuration, which names every role's model.
  - Registry and Settings become sections of one Configuration page (owner decisions O2, O3; DEC-25 R18 kept the settings, not a separate view) — setting up models, comparing them and setting review capacity are one task for a person, and a non-developer must be able to do it without a terminal. The benchmark there compares combinations in two tiers, quick and overnight, and labels as indistinguishable what its intervals cannot separate, because a ranking the measurement cannot support would outrun the evidence (§2.1.6).
  - The Integrations page no longer carries its own catalogue for a 404 — a page-held catalogue drifts from the server's; the API's `tier` keeps the roadmap visible.
  - Card tabs underline in `--text-primary`, not gold — gold stays in its places (three since DEC-51, §2.1.4).
  - Kind labels come from NAMING's one map of the kernel's seven stored kinds, and a split's SPIDR axis from the stored `split` field; the page's own seven-value `CardKind`, which had no Spike or Review and showed a spike as Research, is folded in (DEC-26, review B3).
  - The stored state `in_progress` reads *In progress*, not *In Progress* — sentence case everywhere, stored labels included (review m4).
  - *(Reversed by DEC-35: Inbox is a page again, §2.2.1, §2.17.2, and Review › Needs you stays the project's decision queue.)* Inbox is merged into Review's *Needs you* (its route stays and opens it) — an open decision, a parked card and a card failed after the ladder are all waiting on a person's move, and one queue sorted by wait is the list a reviewer works through; the flat navigation had 14 items (P11).
  - Copy examples use a 40-step budget (*8 of 40 steps*, *step 5 of 40*, *Budget: 40 steps*), not the old mockups' 32 — 40 is the step cap the planner enforces (`INVEST_MAX_STEPS`, [planner-pm §2.4](planner-pm.md)), the one step number a reader meets elsewhere in the design.
  - The verification widths are 1440×900, 1100×800 and 400×812, not 1024×768 and 375×812 — 1100 sits inside the 1024–1279 band whose labelled sidebar is new (§2.2.2), and 400 is the width at which Status must work for a non-developer (§2.8.13).
  - The default route follows the first-run role (§2.2.5) rather than always being Review-or-Board — a non-developer's home is Status, and a beginner's is the Board with Learn on; *I write code* keeps the old rule.
  - Runs and Playbook have no empty state: each is hidden until it has content (§2.2.1), so their old empty copy (with `sekhemet queue --auto-accept`) is gone — a hidden view is never a dead link, and no path ends in a terminal.
  - The old Playbook table (instruction, trigger gate, teaching card, since, pattern) and its *Promote* / *Dismiss* of suggested rules are replaced by the learning view (§2.11) — rules are approved, scoped and credited from the learning store ([DEC-28](../DECISIONS.md#dec-28--one-rule-for-admitting-what-the-system-learns)), not promoted from a candidates file with one trigger gate.
  - A focused tile uses the global focus outline, offset 1 px outside it (§2.14.5), not the old design's ring inset by 1 px — deliberate, for consistency: one focus style on every control, so focus looks the same on a tile as anywhere else; the column body's 8 px padding (§2.4.2) leaves room for it. The code's inset (`board.css:192`) moves under P12 (DB-P12-7).
  - *Closed* is no longer a column: *Won't do* is a filter, not a column ([NAMING](../NAMING.md)); On hold keeps the old design's placement far right and its count in the parked tone (§2.4.3).
  - **D1, after the C1 audit (DEC-51 to DEC-53, DEC-56).** Each conflict below is resolved by the later ruling, as CLAUDE.md requires:
    - The primary button is dark ink, not gold (DEC-51, settling SPEC-07): §2.1.4 and §2.13.7 had said gold while §2.13's standard drew ink; gold now has three places, not four, and card tabs, the Recommended tag and Seshat's monogram stay ungilded as before.
    - The Inbox is two panes at 1100 px and wider and one list below (DEC-51), replacing DEC-35's one grouped list whose rows left the Inbox (§2.17.2).
    - Free words in the query language match full text (DEC-51), not title and key only (§2.4.12); FTS5 in `node:sqlite` replaces DESIGN_GAPS b14's *FTS5 or a scan*.
    - *Assignee* is the on-screen field and `owner:` its alias (DEC-52), reversing NAMING row 22 and DEC-25 R10's *owner* on screen with *assignee* only as an alias; *Delegate* stays.
    - *Request changes*, *Put on hold*, *Take off hold*, *Activity log*, *Needs re-checking*, *Tests too weak*, *Acceptance criteria* and *Files in scope* replace NAMING's and DEC-31's *Send back*, *Park*, *Unpark*, *Ledger*, *Suspect*, *Passing, strength unmet*, *Done when* and *May edit* on screen (DEC-52).
    - *New project* opens the start page with a live draft (DEC-51, b25), not Seshat's panel; and on a server that already holds its project it says how to start another server (DEC-53 c3, [teams](teams.md) item 5), an exception to §2.4.7's *no path ends in the CLI* that DEC-53 states as v1's limitation. *(Its second half is superseded by DEC-57 in D2, below.)*
    - The brand mark's disc sits on the horizon above the gate (DEC-51, the Logo board), not inside it as built since 3410f94.
    - Configuration's *Suggested setup* card is not titled *from Seshat*, as the mockup titles it: the recommendation is a rule's ([models](models.md) NEW-models-12), and §2.1.6 forbids the page to say more than the evidence.
    - Evidence: [FINDINGS_C1](../../reference/FINDINGS_C1.md) (PRC-01, PRC-02, PRC-04, PRC-05, PRC-11, PRC-12, BRD-05, BRD-07, REV-10, STA-08, ISS-01, ISS-04, TEAM-01, TEAM-03, TEAM-04, CFG-07, VIS-02, SPEC-07) and [DESIGN_GAPS_C1](../../reference/DESIGN_GAPS_C1.md) (b4, b6, b14, b15, b19, b20, b21, b22, b24, b25; c3, c4, c12, c14).
  - **D2, after DEC-57 (2026-10-01).** [DEC-57](../DECISIONS.md#dec-57--a-server-is-a-workspace-with-many-projects) → §2.2's Project row and items 6 and 7, §2.8 item 11, §2.11's Projects, DB-N9-11 and -12 amended, NEW-dashboard-25 (DB-N25-1 to -4, which fix FINDINGS SHL-01) and NEW-dashboard-26 (DB-N26-1 to -3), and the §7 entry withdrawn. **Resolved by the later ruling:** D1 said that a server holds one project and that New project says how to start another server (DEC-53 c3), and the account menu offered *Switch workspace* in the Team setup only; DEC-57 is the later ruling, so New project works in any workspace, and *Switch workspace* is in both setups. Both switchers sit where the approved mockups draw them: the project switcher in the sidebar's Project group, *Switch workspace* in the account menu (`gen/common.py`'s `sidebar` and `account_menu`; DEC-51). Research: Linear's workspace whose guests see only the teams they are added to, and Jira's per-space access within one site ([DESIGN_RESEARCH_COLLABORATION.md](../../research/DESIGN_RESEARCH_COLLABORATION.md) §1).
